309: ## Bottom Line
310: 
311: 出力保持、トークン削減、Invariants 完全。さもなくば revert。
312: 
313: Ratchet は前にしか進まない。

FILE skill-refactor/README.md
1: # Skill Refactor
2: 
3: **English** | [日本語](README.ja.md) | [繁體中文](README.zh-TW.md)
4: 
5: > Token / structure refactor for an existing skill — output
6: > behavior preserved, equivalence proven by multi-judge ensemble +
7: > structured comparison, ratcheted via git revert on regression.
8: 
9: A user-invoked **gate skill**: when an existing skill's `SKILL.md`
10: has accumulated tokens / cruft and you want to shrink it without
11: changing what the skill does, you invoke this skill. It enforces
12: output equivalence as a hard precondition before any token-saving
13: edit is committed.
14: 
15: This README is for humans reading the skill on GitHub. The
16: operational file Claude actually loads is [`SKILL.md`](SKILL.md).
17: 
18: ---
19: 
20: ## Why does this skill exist?
21: 
22: **The recurring failure mode**: skills accumulate tokens. SKILL.md
23: files grow over rounds of edits. Most edits are additive — fixing a
24: corner case here, adding an example there. The result: skills that
25: take more tokens to load than they need to, with output behavior
26: that's the same as before (or worse — longer prompts can degrade
27: focus).
28: 
29: Without an explicit gate, every edit defaults to *additive*. This
30: skill captures the discipline that catches the additive default
31: specifically for token / structure work, while ruling out behavior
32: change as out-of-scope.
33: 
34: The discipline is three checks:
35: 
36: 1. **Output equivalence** — proven by 3-judge ensemble + structured
37:    comparison, not asserted by the editor
38: 2. **Token reduction ≥10%** — cosmetic micro-edits don't earn
39:    ratchet credit
40: 3. **Invariants preserved** — name / dependencies / contract / file
41:    structure unchanged
42: 
43: If any of three fails, `git revert`. The **ratchet only turns
44: forward**.
45: 
46: ---
47: 
48: ## How does it work?
49: 
50: ### Operational flow at a glance
51: 
52: ```mermaid
53: flowchart TD
54:     A([User invokes skill on existing skill X]) --> B[Phase 0: Capture baseline]
55:     B --> B1[Run test-prompts.json on current SKILL.md]
56:     B --> B2[Capture token count + invariants]
57:     B1 --> C[Phase 1: Propose refactor move]
58:     B2 --> C
59:     C --> D[Apply move + git commit]
60:     D --> Q1{Q1: Output equivalent?}
61:     Q1 -->|FAIL| R[git revert + record fail]
62:     Q1 -->|FLAG| H[Tier 2 escalate to human]
63:     Q1 -->|PASS| Q2{Q2: Token reduction ≥10%?}
64:     Q2 -->|≥10%| Q3{Q3: Invariants intact?}
65:     Q2 -->|5% to below 10%| WC[RESHAPE: weak win, user decides]
66:     Q2 -->|<5% or increase| R
67:     Q3 -->|PASS| K[PROCEED — keep, ratchet advances]
68:     Q3 -->|FAIL| R
69:     R --> NEXT[next round or skill]
70:     K --> NEXT
71:     WC --> NEXT
72:     H --> NEXT
73: ```
74: 
75: ### The three checks
76: 
77: | Check | Mechanism | Failure → |
78: |---|---|---|
79: | **Q1 Equivalence** | Layer 1 structural (deterministic) + Layer 2 LLM-judge ensemble (3 calls, varied framing) | REJECT or FLAG (Tier 2) |
80: | **Q2 Token reduction** | `wc -w` before vs after; ≥10% threshold | REJECT (or RESHAPE if 5% to below 10%) |
81: | **Q3 Invariants** | Name / dependencies / contract / structure unchanged | REJECT |
82: 
83: ### The verdict vocabulary
84: 
85: Parallel to loom-workflow's critique skills:
86: 
87: | Verdict | When | Action |
88: |---|---|---|
89: | **PROCEED** | All 3 checks strict pass | Ratchet advances; commit kept |
90: | **RESHAPE** | Weak Q2 (5% to below 10% reduction) but Q1+Q3 pass | Show user; decide keep or further-refactor |
91: | **REJECT** | Any of Q1/Q2/Q3 fails | `git revert`; record in results.tsv; move on |
92: | (Tier 2) | Q1 = `FLAG_TIER_2` | Escalate to human review |
93: 
94: ### Multi-judge ensemble (the load-bearing innovation)
95: 
96: LLM-as-judge has known failure modes (verbosity bias, position bias,
97: self-preference, insensitivity to subtle change). Single-judge
98: verdicts on equivalence are unreliable. This skill runs 3 judges
99: with varied prompt framing:
100: 
101: | Judge | Frame | What it catches |
102: |---|---|---|
103: | 1 | "Same value to user" (utility) | Structural preservation |
104: | 2 | "Same information set" (content) | Silent content drops |
105: | 3 | "Same edge-case handling" (boundary) | Lost fallbacks / warnings |
106: 
107: Plus: **specific-behavior-diff override**. A single judge citing a
108: *concrete* behavior change outranks 2/3 numeric majority. This is
109: what catches refactors that smuggle feature work.
110: 
111: Full protocol: [`references/multi-judge-ensemble.md`](references/multi-judge-ensemble.md).
112: 
113: ---
114: 
115: ## When should you use it?
116: 
117: ### Invoke when…
118: 
119: - An existing skill's SKILL.md is too long / repetitive / cruft-y
120:   and you want to trim
121: - You typed something like:
122:   - "shorten this skill"
123:   - "reduce token count"
124:   - "縮減 SKILL.md"
125:   - "整理 skill 結構"
126:   - "refactor without changing behavior"
127:   - "リファクタ skill / トークン削減"
128: - The skill you want to refactor has (or can have) a
129:   `test-prompts.json` with ≥3 representative prompts
130: - You explicitly want output behavior preserved
131: 
132: ### Don't invoke when…
133: 
134: - **Skill output is bad / wrong** — use
135:   `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning` — taste
136:   improvement requires human-judged A/B, not equivalence-preserving
137:   refactor
138: - **You want to add a phase / change agent / restructure
139:   workflow** — use `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-creator-advance` — structural
140:   rewrite is feature-hat work
141: - **Creating a new skill** — use `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-creator-advance`
142: - **Single-line cosmetic edits** — just edit directly; the gate cost
143:   exceeds the change cost
144: - **Skill has no `test-prompts.json` and user won't write one** —
145:   the equivalence check can't run; either provide test prompts or
146:   use `skill-creator-advance` to redesign with proper test infra
147: - **Skill output is creative / non-deterministic** (writing style,
148:   prose, design feel) — equivalence check unreliable; use
149:   `skill-tuning`
150: 
151: ---
152: 
153: ## What does the output look like?
154: 
155: ### Worked Example — token bloat in skill-creator-advance
156: 
157: **Input**: User says "skill-creator-advance is at 5627 words, way
158: over the soft cap. Refactor it."
159: 
160: **Phase 0 (baseline)**:
161: - Capture `test-prompts.json` (3 prompts: creation / improvement /
162:   description optimization)
163: - Run baseline against current skill → outputs in `<workspace>/baseline/`
164: - Entrypoint count: 5627 words; capture whole-package baseline in package mode
165: - Invariant snapshot: `name` / dependencies / structure recorded
166: 
167: **Phase 1, Round 1**:
168: - Move: extract Description Optimization section (~700 words) to
169:   `references/description-optimization.md`
170: - Q1 ensemble (illustrative): 3/3 say outputs equivalent (description-opt use case
171:   still works because reference loads on demand)
172: - Q2: entrypoint 5627 → 4927 = 12.4% smaller, but the ~700 words
173:   remain in the reference. Whole-package words stay the same or increase
174:   with the pointer; moving prose alone earns no reduction.
175: - Q3: name and existing dependencies preserved; the new bundled reference
176:   is required for equivalent extraction (allowed in package mode) ✓
177: - **Verdict: REJECT** — discard this candidate: whole-package Q2 fails
178:   even if Q1 and Q3 pass.
179: 
180: A later candidate must remove actual duplication and independently pass
181: Q1/Q2/Q3 on the whole package; no later rounds are verified here.
182: 
183: ### Worked Example — refactor that should be REJECT
184: 
185: **Input**: User says "let me try this rewording — it's clearer".
186: 
187: - Q1 result: 2/3 judges say outputs equivalent; 1 dissents — "the
188:   candidate's output skips the file-permission check that the
189:   baseline performed"
190: - Q2 result: 5% reduction
191: - Q3 result: clean
192: 
193: The dissenting judge's reason cites a **specific behavior change**.
194: This triggers the specific-behavior-diff override even though 2/3
195: voted equivalent.
196: 
197: **Verdict: FLAG → user reviews dissent**
198: 
199: User examines and confirms — yes, the "clearer" rewording dropped a
200: phrase that nudged Claude to perform the file-permission check.
201: Subtle behavior change masked as refactor.
202: 
203: → **REJECT this round; do not commit.** This is what the
204: multi-judge ensemble exists to catch.
205: 
206: ---
207: 
208: ## How does it relate to other skills?
209: 
210: This skill operates on **a single existing skill, with output
211: preserved**. Hand off when:
212: 
213: - **a proposal-triage gate** — when faced with multiple
214:   refactor proposals, triage which to do first
215: - **a complexity / deletion-first gate** — when the question is
216:   "should we even refactor this skill at all" (smallest-end-state
217:   thinking before invoking refactor)
218: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-creator-advance`** — when the change is
219:   structural (add phase, change agents, redesign workflow)
220: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning`** — when the
221:   question turns from "are outputs equivalent" to "which output is
222:   better"
223: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-judge`** — optional advisory check before /
224:   after refactor; if score drops while equivalence keeps passing,
225:   signal of subtle taste-drift the equivalence check missed
226: 
227: ---
228: 
229: ## Where in skill-dev-toolkit does this fit?
230: 
231: The skill-authoring lifecycle (all in `skill-dev-toolkit`):
232: 
233: - `skill-creator-advance` — creation + redesign
234: - `skill-judge` — advisory design score
235: - `skill-refactor` — Phase A: token / structure refactor, output preserved
236: - `skill-tuning` — Phase B: output A/B, human judge, preference log
237: - `dogfood-skill-testing` — blind behavioral test
238: 
239: The general critique gates (`proposal-critique` / `complexity-critique`)
240: stay in `loom-workflow`.
241: 
242: The split between `skill-refactor` (Phase A) and `skill-tuning`
243: (Phase B) reflects Fowler's Two Hats applied to skills: refactor
244: preserves behavior, tuning changes it. They are deliberately
245: separate skills to prevent rubric-mixing that LLM-as-judge cannot
246: reliably handle.
247: 
248: ---
249: 
250: ## Origin / lineage
251: 
252: This skill is **original design**, not a port or fork.
253: 
254: The "autonomous loop with git ratchet" concept was popularized by
255: [`alchaincyf/darwin-skill`](https://github.com/alchaincyf/darwin-skill)
256: (MIT), which was itself inspired by Andrej Karpathy's
257: [`autoresearch`](https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch).
258: 
259: **Why this is original, not derivative**: `darwin-skill` mixes
260: structural refactoring with output quality evaluation in a single
261: 8-dim rubric. This skill (skill-refactor) deliberately handles only
262: Phase A (structure with output preservation); Phase B (output
263: quality A/B) is the separate `skill-tuning` skill. The split avoids
264: the LLM-as-judge / Goodhart drift that monolithic taste-rubrics
265: produce.
266: 
267: Other distinctions: 3-judge ensemble + varied framing (vs single
268: judge), specific-behavior-diff override (vs majority rules), three
269: concrete questions (vs 8-dim weighted score), Tier 1/2/3 cascade
270: (vs binary keep/revert).
271: 
272: Full design-influence detail in [`NOTICE`](NOTICE).
273: 
274: ---
275: 
276: ## Known limitations
277: 
278: | Limitation | What it means | Mitigation |
279: |---|---|---|
280: | **Requires test-prompts.json** | Skills without ≥3 documented test prompts can't run the gate. | Skill self-aborts and asks user to write prompts (or use skill-creator-advance to redesign with test infra). |
281: | **LLM-judge is not infallible** | Even 3-judge ensemble can miss subtle behavior changes. | Specific-behavior-diff override + Tier 2 human escalation + (optional) golden anchor anchoring. |
282: | **Token-only metric is coarse** | A 30-line type-system trick may be denser than 100 lines of straightforward prose. | The 10% reduction threshold prevents tiny-win refactors; substantial refactors are visible. |
283: | **Output preservation is ill-defined for creative skills** | Skills producing prose / writing style have no objective "equivalent". | Self-aborts and recommends `skill-tuning` for those skills. |
284: | **Cumulative drift across rounds** | 3 consecutive moderate-confidence PROCEEDs can compound subtle drift. | Auto-flag for human review of cumulative diff after 3 such rounds. |
285: | **Validation not yet performed** | This skill ships before dry-run validation against ≥2 real skills. | Validation gate per architecture doc §6 is OUTSTANDING; PR-2 ships with this caveat noted in PR description. |
286: 
287: ---
288:

FILE skill-refactor/README.zh-TW.md
1: # Skill Refactor
2: 
3: [English](README.md) | [日本語](README.ja.md) | **繁體中文**
4: 
5: > 對既有 skill 進行 token / 結構重構 — 行為保留、用 multi-judge
6: > ensemble + 結構化比對證明等價、退步自動 git revert。
7: 
8: 由使用者主動呼叫的 **gate skill**：當既有 skill 的 `SKILL.md` 累積
9: 過多 token / 雜訊，想要在**不改變 skill 行為**的前提下縮短 / 整理，
10: 呼叫此 skill。它強制把「輸出等價」當成 hard precondition，任何
11: token-saving 編輯需先通過等價檢查才能 commit。
12: 
13: 本 README 給在 GitHub 上閱讀的人類。Claude 實際載入的 operational
14: 檔案是 [`SKILL.md`](SKILL.md)。
15: 
16: ---
17: 
18: ## 為什麼存在這個 skill？
19: 
20: **反覆出現的失敗模式**：skill 會累積 token。SKILL.md 在多輪編輯
21: 中變大。多數編輯是 additive — 補一個 corner case、加一個範例。結
22: 果：skill 載入要的 token 比真正需要的多，輸出行為跟之前一樣
23: （或更差 — 長 prompt 反而稀釋 focus）。
24: 
25: 沒有明確的 gate，每次編輯預設是 *additive*。這個 skill 把抓取
26: additive default 的紀律凝結，**專門處理 token / 結構工作**，並把
27: 行為改變排除在 scope 外。
28: 
29: 紀律是三項檢查：
30: 
31: 1. **輸出等價** — 由 3-judge ensemble + 結構化比對證明，不是編輯者
32:    主張
33: 2. **Token 縮減 ≥10%** — 美化型微調拿不到 ratchet 信用
34: 3. **Invariants 保留** — name / dependencies / contract / 檔案結構
35:    不變
36: 
37: 任一失敗 → `git revert`。**Ratchet 只能往前**。
38: 
39: ---
40: 
41: ## 它如何運作？
42: 
43: ### Operational flow 一覽
44: 
45: ```mermaid
46: flowchart TD
47:     A([使用者對既有 skill X 呼叫]) --> B[Phase 0: 建立 baseline]
48:     B --> B1[對當前 SKILL.md 跑 test-prompts.json]
49:     B --> B2[紀錄 token count + invariants]
50:     B1 --> C[Phase 1: 提出 refactor move]
51:     B2 --> C
52:     C --> D[套用 move + git commit]
53:     D --> Q1{Q1: 輸出等價?}
54:     Q1 -->|FAIL| R[git revert + 紀錄失敗]
55:     Q1 -->|FLAG| H[Tier 2 升給人類]
56:     Q1 -->|PASS| Q2{Q2: Token 縮減 ≥10%?}
57:     Q2 -->|≥10%| Q3{Q3: Invariants 完好?}
58:     Q2 -->|5%以上、未滿10%| WC[RESHAPE: 微弱 win, user 決定]
59:     Q2 -->|<5% 或增加| R
60:     Q3 -->|PASS| K[PROCEED — 留下, ratchet 推進]
61:     Q3 -->|FAIL| R
62:     R --> NEXT[下一輪 or 下一 skill]
63:     K --> NEXT
64:     WC --> NEXT
65:     H --> NEXT
66: ```
67: 
68: ### 三項檢查
69: 
70: | 檢查 | 機制 | 失敗 → |
71: |---|---|---|
72: | **Q1 等價性** | Layer 1 結構（決定性）+ Layer 2 LLM-judge ensemble（3 call, 不同框架）| REJECT 或 FLAG (Tier 2) |
73: | **Q2 Token 縮減** | `wc -w` 前後比；≥10% 門檻 | REJECT（5%以上、未滿10% 為 RESHAPE）|
74: | **Q3 Invariants** | name / dependencies / contract / 結構不變 | REJECT |
75: 
76: ### Verdict 詞彙
77: 
78: 跟 loom-workflow 的 critique skill 平行：
79: 
80: | Verdict | 條件 | 動作 |
81: |---|---|---|
82: | **PROCEED** | 三項全部嚴格 pass | Ratchet 推進、commit 留下 |
83: | **RESHAPE** | Q2 微弱（5%以上、未滿10%）但 Q1+Q3 過 | 給 user 看、user 決定留或繼續 refactor |
84: | **REJECT** | Q1/Q2/Q3 任一失敗 | `git revert`、記 results.tsv、繼續 |
85: | (Tier 2) | Q1 = `FLAG_TIER_2` | 升給人類 review |
86: 
87: ### Multi-judge ensemble（核心 innovation）
88: 
89: LLM-as-judge 有已知失敗模式（verbosity bias、position bias、
90: self-preference、subtle 變化不敏）。單 judge 對等價性的判斷不可靠。
91: 此 skill 跑 3 個不同 framing 的 judge：
92: 
93: | Judge | Frame | 抓什麼 |
94: |---|---|---|
95: | 1 | 「對 user 是否同價值」（utility）| 結構保留 |
96: | 2 | 「資訊集合是否相同」（content）| 內容靜默漏失 |
97: | 3 | 「邊界處理是否相同」（boundary）| 失去的 fallback / warning |
98: 
99: 加上：**specific-behavior-diff override**。單一 judge 指出**具體**
100: 行為改變時，即使 2/3 多數說等價，也會 override 多數。這是抓「以
101: refactor 包裝行為改變」的關鍵。
102: 
103: 完整 protocol：[`references/multi-judge-ensemble.md`](references/multi-judge-ensemble.md)。
104: 
105: ---
106: 
107: ## 何時該使用？
108: 
109: ### 在以下情境呼叫…
110: 
111: - 既有 skill 的 SKILL.md 太長 / 重複 / 雜訊多，想要精簡
112: - 你打了類似這樣的話：
113:   - 「縮短這個 skill」
114:   - 「reduce token count」
115:   - 「縮減 SKILL.md」
116:   - 「整理 skill 結構」
117:   - 「保留行為的前提下 refactor」
118:   - 「リファクタ skill / トークン削減」
119: - 要 refactor 的 skill 有（或可建立）`test-prompts.json` 含 ≥3 個
120:   代表性 prompt
121: - 你**明確想要**輸出行為保留
122: 
123: ### 在以下情境**不**呼叫…
124: 
125: - **Skill 輸出不好 / 錯誤** — 用 `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning`
126:   — 品質改進需人類 A/B，不是等價保留 refactor
127: - **想加 phase / 改 agent / 重組 workflow** — 用
128:   `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-creator-advance` — 結構性重寫是 feature hat
129: - **建立新 skill** — 用 `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-creator-advance`
130: - **單行 cosmetic 編輯** — 直接改；gate 成本超過改動成本
131: - **Skill 沒 `test-prompts.json` 且 user 不願寫** — 等價檢查無法
132:   跑；給 prompt 或用 `skill-creator-advance` 重設計
133: - **Skill 輸出是創意 / 非決定性**（寫作風格、文案、設計感受）—
134:   等價檢查不可靠；用 `skill-tuning`
135: 
136: ---
137: 
138: ## 輸出長什麼樣？
139: 
140: ### Worked Example — skill-creator-advance 的 token 膨脹
141: 
142: **Input**：使用者「skill-creator-advance 已經 5627 字、嚴重超過
143: soft cap，refactor 一下」。
144: 
145: **Phase 0（baseline）**：
146: - 建立 `test-prompts.json`（3 個 prompt：創建 / 改進 / description
147:   optimization）
148: - 對當前 skill 跑 baseline → outputs 落 `<workspace>/baseline/`
149: - 入口字數：5627 字；以 package mode 記錄整個套件的 baseline
150: - Invariant snapshot：`name` / 依賴 / 結構紀錄下來
151: 
152: **Phase 1, Round 1**：
153: - Move：把 Description Optimization section（~700 字）抽到
154:   `references/description-optimization.md`
155: - Q1 ensemble（假設結果）：3/3 say 輸出等價（description-opt 使用情境仍
156:   work，因為 reference 按需載入）
157: - Q2：入口 5627 → 4927 = 12.4% 縮小，但 ~700 字仍留在 reference。
158:   整個套件字數不變，或因參照連結而增加；單純搬移文字不算縮減。
159: - Q3：保留 name 與既有依賴；等價抽取所需的新 bundled reference
160:   在 package mode 中允許加入 ✓
161: - **Verdict: REJECT** — 即使 Q1、Q3 通過，whole-package Q2 仍不通過，
162:   因此捨棄這個候選版本。
163: 
164: 下一個候選版本須刪除實際重複，並以整個套件獨立通過 Q1/Q2/Q3；
165: 此例未提供後續輪次的驗證結果。
166: 
167: ### Worked Example — 應該 REJECT 的 refactor
168: 
169: **Input**：使用者「我試試這個改寫 — 比較清楚」。
170: 
171: - Q1 結果：2/3 judges say 輸出等價；1 反對 — 「candidate 的輸出
172:   跳過了 baseline 會做的 file-permission 檢查」
173: - Q2 結果：5% 縮減
174: - Q3 結果：乾淨
175: 
176: 反對 judge 的理由指出**具體行為改變**。即使 2/3 投等價，這啟動
177: specific-behavior-diff override。
178: 
179: **Verdict: FLAG → user 看反對意見**
180: 
181: User 檢查確認 — 是的，那個「比較清楚」的改寫漏掉了一句話，這句
182: 話原本提醒 Claude 做 file-permission 檢查。Refactor 包裝下的細微
183: 行為改變。
184: 
185: → **REJECT 這輪、不 commit**。這是 multi-judge ensemble 存在的
186: 理由。
187: 
188: ---
189: 
190: ## 它與其他 skill 的關係？
191: 
192: 此 skill 處理 **單一既有 skill、保留行為**。需要轉手時：
193: 
194: - **a proposal-triage gate** — 多個 refactor 提案要 triage
195:   時，先決定哪個先做
196: - **a complexity / deletion-first gate** — 問題是「該不該 refactor
197:   這個 skill」時（smallest-end-state 思考再呼叫 refactor）
198: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-creator-advance`** — 改動是結構性的（加
199:   phase / 改 agent / 重設計）
200: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning`** — 問題從「輸出等價
201:   嗎」變成「哪個輸出更好」時
202: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-judge`** — 可選 advisory；refactor 前 / 後
203:   跑分；如果分數降但等價檢查持續通過，是細微 taste drift 的訊號
204: 
205: ---
206: 
207: ## 在 skill-dev-toolkit 中的位置
208: 
209: skill 撰寫的生命週期（全部在 `skill-dev-toolkit`）：
210: 
211: - `skill-creator-advance` — 建立 + 重設計
212: - `skill-judge` — advisory 設計評分
213: - `skill-refactor` — Phase A: token / 結構重構，行為保留
214: - `skill-tuning` — Phase B: 輸出 A/B、人類 judge、preference log
215: - `dogfood-skill-testing` — 盲測行為
216: 
217: 通用 critique gate（`proposal-critique` / `complexity-critique`）
218: 留在 `loom-workflow`。
219: 
220: `skill-refactor`（Phase A）跟 `skill-tuning`（Phase B）的拆分
221: 反映 Fowler Two Hats 套到 skill：refactor 保留行為、tuning 改變
222: 行為。**故意分開**避免 rubric-mixing 讓 LLM-as-judge 可靠處理不了
223: 的問題。
224: 
225: ---
226: 
227: ## Origin / lineage
228: 
229: 此 skill 是 **獨立設計**，不是 port 或 fork。
230: 
231: 「自主迴圈 + git ratchet」的概念由
232: [`alchaincyf/darwin-skill`](https://github.com/alchaincyf/darwin-skill)
233: （MIT）推廣，後者本身啟發自 Andrej Karpathy 的
234: [`autoresearch`](https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch)。
235: 
236: **為何是獨立而非衍生**：`darwin-skill` 把結構 refactor 跟輸出品質
237: 評估混在單一 8 維 rubric。此 skill（skill-refactor）刻意只處理
238: Phase A（結構 + 行為保留）；Phase B（輸出品質 A/B）是另一個獨立
239: skill `skill-tuning`。這個拆分避開了單一 rubric 帶 LLM-as-judge /
240: Goodhart drift 的問題。
241: 
242: 其他差異：3-judge ensemble + varied framing（vs 單 judge）、
243: specific-behavior-diff override（vs 多數決）、三項具體問題（vs
244: 8 維加權分）、Tier 1/2/3 cascade（vs 二元 keep/revert）。
245: 
246: 完整 design-influence 細節見 [`NOTICE`](NOTICE)。
247: 
248: ---
249: 
250: ## 已知限制
251: 
252: | 限制 | 意義 | 緩解 |
253: |---|---|---|
254: | **需要 test-prompts.json** | 沒有 ≥3 個文件化 test prompt 的 skill 跑不了 gate | Skill 自動 abort 並請 user 撰寫（或用 skill-creator-advance 重設計含 test infra）|
255: | **LLM-judge 不是萬無一失** | 即使 3-judge ensemble 仍可能漏失細微行為改變 | Specific-behavior-diff override + Tier 2 人類升級 +（可選）golden anchor 錨定 |
256: | **Token-only metric 粒度粗** | 30 行 type-system 技巧可能比 100 行直白文字密 | 10% 縮減門檻防 tiny-win refactor；實質 refactor 看得見 |
257: | **創意 skill 的「輸出保留」定義不清** | 寫作風格 / 散文 skill 沒有客觀「等價」 | 自動 abort 並推薦 `skill-tuning` |
258: | **跨輪累積 drift** | 連續 3 個 moderate-confidence PROCEED 可能複合細微 drift | 連續 3 輪 moderate-confidence 後自動 flag 累積 diff 給人類 review |
259: | **未經驗證即發布** | 此 skill 在 ≥2 個真實 skill 的 dry-run validation 之前 ship | 架構 doc §6 的 validation gate 為 OUTSTANDING；PR-2 在 PR description 標註此 caveat |
260: 
261: ---

FILE skill-refactor/SKILL.md
1: ---
2: name: skill-refactor
3: description: |
4:   Token/structure refactor of a skill, preserving output behavior — checks equivalence, ≥10% token cut, invariants. Use for 'shorten this skill', 'reduce tokens', '縮減 SKILL.md'. Output A/B → skill-tuning; redesign/create → skill-creator-advance.
5: ---
6: 
7: # Skill Refactor
8: 
9: A user-invoked gate skill: forces any proposed refactor of an
10: existing skill — token reduction, structure cleanup, dedupe, prose
11: tightening — through a deletion-first design pass with **automated
12: output equivalence verification** before the change is committed.
13: 
14: ## Overview
15: 
16: Skills accumulate tokens. SKILL.md files grow over rounds of
17: edits. Most edits are additive — fixing a corner case here, adding
18: an example there. The result: skills that take more tokens to load
19: than they need to, with output behavior that's the same as before
20: (or worse, since longer prompts can degrade focus).
21: 
22: **This skill is the refactor hat applied to skills**: improve
23: internal structure without changing external behavior. Output
24: equivalence is enforced by a multi-judge ensemble + structured
25: comparison; behavior changes are out of scope (route to
26: `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning` for output quality work or
27: `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-creator-advance` for structural redesign).
28: 
29: This skill is **runtime self-contained** — `skill-dev-toolkit` is the
30: only plugin needed. No cross-plugin dependency.
31: 
32: ## The Iron Law
33: 
34: ```
35: NO REFACTOR AUTO-SHIPS WITHOUT (1) EQUIVALENCE PROVEN
36:                                (2) TOKEN REDUCTION ≥10%
37:                                (3) INVARIANTS PRESERVED
38: ```
39: 
40: If either behavior or invariant preservation fails, discard the candidate. A
41: reduction of at least 5% and below 10% may ship only after explicit user
42: acceptance of the marginal win; it does not auto-advance the ratchet. Only refactors that **measurably
43: preserve behavior while measurably shrinking the file** survive.
44: 
45: ## Before You Begin — Establish Baseline
46: 
47: Before proposing any refactor, the target skill needs:
48: 
49: 1. **`test-prompts.json`** — at least 3 representative prompts that
50:    cover the skill's documented use cases. Schema:
51:    `references/test-prompts-schema.md`. If the skill doesn't have
52:    one, create it (with user confirmation) before any refactor.
53: 2. **A baseline run** — execute each test prompt against the
54:    *current* SKILL.md via subagent; capture outputs to
55:    `<workspace>/baseline/`. This is what "equivalence" is checked
56:    against.
57: 3. **Baseline token count** — `wc -w` of the current SKILL.md. Used
58:    for Q2's threshold check.
59: 4. **Invariant snapshot** — record current frontmatter `name`,
60:    non-description fields, declared dependencies, file structure
61:    (subdirectories, key bundled files). This is what Q3 protects.
62: 
63: **Do not edit a candidate or propose Q1's refactor moves until baseline is captured.**
64: 
65: Use **entrypoint mode** when only `SKILL.md` changes. Use package-resource
66: mode only when the candidate changes, adds, or removes a bundled resource; then read
67: `references/package-resource-mode.md` before creating the candidate.
68: 
69: **PACKAGE-MODE LOAD GATE.** For any bundled-resource request, before
70: explaining, planning, or capturing a baseline, use the host's file-reading
71: capability to read `references/package-resource-mode.md` whole. If that read
72: is unavailable or fails, STOP with `UNGRADABLE`. Do not reconstruct package
73: mode from this entrypoint or continue from memory.
74: 
75: ### Round scope
76: 
77: - **Entrypoint mode: split a round** when it would change `SKILL.md` and a
78:   bundled resource; retain the one-file-per-round rule.
79: - **Package mode: one isolated package round may change** its target and
80:   **directly supporting bundled resources** when the package protocol needs
81:   them together for equivalent behavior. Unrelated bundled resources remain
82:   split into later rounds.
83: 
84: If any of (1)-(4) cannot be obtained (e.g., skill has no
85: deterministic test prompts because output is purely creative), the
86: gate cannot run safely. Report the blocker to the user and suggest
87: `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning` if the underlying intent was quality
88: improvement, or manual edit if only cosmetic.
89: 
90: ## The Gate Function (per round)
91: 
92: Each refactor round runs three independent checks against the
93: candidate (post-edit) SKILL.md.
94: 
95: ### Q1. Output equivalence (multi-judge ensemble)
96: 
97: For each test prompt:
98: - Run candidate SKILL.md via subagent → capture output
99: - Compare to baseline output via:
100:   - **Structural check** (cheap, deterministic): same key sections /
101:     headings / file paths / tool calls present
102:   - **LLM-judge ensemble** (3 independent calls with varied prompt
103:     framing, see `references/multi-judge-ensemble.md`): is the
104:     semantic content equivalent?
105: 
106: **Pass rule**: structural OK and `PASS_HIGH` or `PASS_MODERATE` from the
107: existing reducer (`scripts/multi_judge.py`):
108: 
109: | Ensemble result (apply in this order) | Q1 outcome |
110: |---|---|
111: | Specific behavior dissent from any `not_equivalent` judge | `FLAG_TIER_2` — escalate before considering vote counts |
112: | 3 `equivalent` | `PASS_HIGH` |
113: | 2 `equivalent` + 1 `uncertain` | `PASS_MODERATE` — report moderate confidence; may proceed |
114: | 2 `equivalent` + 1 `not_equivalent` | `FLAG_TIER_2` — human review; hold candidate |
115: | ≤1 `equivalent`, after the dissent override | `FAIL` — reject |
116: 
117: **Pass = output is functionally the same as before.** This is the
118: load-bearing guarantee of refactor work.
119: 
120: ### Q2. Entrypoint-mode token reduction ≥10%
121: 
122: Entrypoint mode measures `wc -w` on candidate `SKILL.md` compared to baseline:
123: 
124: | Reduction | Verdict |
125: |---|---|
126: | ≥10% reduction | OK |
127: | 5% ≤ reduction <10% | OK only if Q1 + Q3 also strictly pass; weak win |
128: | <5% reduction (or no reduction) | **REJECT** — refactor cost not worth it |
129: | **Increase** | **REJECT** — this isn't a refactor; route elsewhere |
130: 
131: The 10% threshold prevents cosmetic micro-edits from accumulating
132: ratchet credit. Each refactor round must be a real win.
133: Package mode uses whole-package Q2 under `references/package-resource-mode.md`;
134: moving prose into a resource alone earns no reduction.
135: 
136: ### Q3. Invariant preservation
137: 
138: Compare candidate against the snapshot captured in §Before You
139: Begin:
140: 
141: | Invariant | Allowed change |
142: |---|---|
143: | `name` (frontmatter) | Never change |
144: | `description` (frontmatter) | Allowed; this is often the target of token reduction |
145: | Other frontmatter fields (e.g., `compatibility`) | Never change |
146: | Declared dependencies (scripts called, references referenced) | Preserve existing dependencies; allow a new bundled reference required for equivalent extraction in package mode. Other dependency changes go to skill-creator-advance. |
147: | Subdirectory structure (`agents/`, `references/`, `scripts/`) | Allowed to add files (e.g. extract content); never to remove without skill-creator-advance |
148: | Bundled file *contents* | Allowed. Entrypoint mode: each cascade is its own round. Package mode follows §Round scope: one isolated package round may include target + directly supporting bundled resources; unrelated resources split. |
149: 
150: **Pass rule**: all "never change" items unchanged; "allowed" items
151: verified to still serve the skill's documented behavior.
152: 
153: ## Verdict
154: 
155: After Q1, Q2, Q3:
156: 
157: - **PROCEED** — all three pass strictly. Apply the isolated candidate to the
158:   user's worktree, then `git commit` it; ratchet advances.
159: - **RESHAPE** — Q1 and Q3 pass, Q2 marginal (5% ≤ reduction <10%). Show
160:   user the candidate + which dimension is weak; user decides keep
161:   or further-refactor. Do not auto-merge.
162: - **REJECT** — Q1 fails (output not equivalent), or Q2 shows
163:   increase / no reduction, or Q3 violated. Discard the isolated
164:   candidate; record the failed move in `results.tsv`; move to next
165:   round (or skill, if this skill has hit its bound).
166: 
167: ## Ablation mode — find bloat without a baseline
168: 
169: The Gate Function proves a *rewrite* preserves behavior. **Ablation mode** answers
170: a different question with the same infra — *which sections add words without adding
171: capability* — and needs **no external baseline** (the skill is its own control; you
172: don't need an ancestor or sibling to compare against).
173: 
174: **Procedure** (reuses `test-prompts.json` + the Q1 judge ensemble):
175: 
176: 1. Run the test prompts through the FULL skill → baseline outputs.
177: 2. For each section, generate a variant with **that one section removed**; re-run the prompts.
178: 3. Judge ensemble compares full-vs-ablated per section:
179:    - behavior **unchanged** on removal → **BLOAT candidate** (compress / cut);
180:    - behavior **degraded** (weaker refusal, dropped step, wrong decision) → **LOAD-BEARING** (keep).
181: 
182: **Three caveats — ship them with every ablation report:**
183: 
184: 1. **Redundancy trap.** Two overlapping sections each read as removable (the other
185:    covers for it), but removing both may break behavior. Disagreement across two
186:    related sections suggests overlap; test a merged candidate through Q1/Q2/Q3.
187: 2. **Coverage boundary.** A section reads as bloat if no test prompt exercises its
188:    purpose. Navigational sections (`See also`, cross-skill tables) carry discoverability
189:    value that behavioral ablation cannot measure — judge those by hand.
190: 3. **Candidate-finder, not auto-deleter.** Ablation ranks refactor *targets*; the actual
191:    cut still passes the Q1/Q2/Q3 gate.
192: 
193: Grounded in ablation-study methodology (generic prompt additions are non-monotonic —
194: validate each component against task evals, not intuition) and the context-rot finding
195: that surplus tokens measurably *degrade* output, so a confirmed-bloat cut is often
196: net-positive, not neutral. See `references/ablation-mode.md` for the section-splitter +
197: worked example.
198: 
199: ## Refactor Moves Catalog
200: 
201: Refactor-hat-safe transformations only. See
202: `references/refactor-moves-catalog.md` for full list with examples.
203: Quick reference:
204: 
205: | Move | Effect | Equivalence risk |
206: |---|---|---|
207: | Dedupe prose | Same content stated once | Low |
208: | Extract to `references/` | Move detail out of SKILL.md body | Medium (if reference loaded on-demand correctly) |
209: | Compress lists / tables | Replace bullet duplication with concise table | Low |
210: | Inline single-use definition | Remove indirection | Low |
211: | Tighten verbose phrasing | "in order to" → "to" | Low |
212: | Remove "ALWAYS / NEVER" caps where the prose can carry meaning | Reduce noise | Low–medium (test for behavior change) |
213: | Move worked examples to companion file | If 3+ examples, extract per domain-team structural gates companion-file pattern | Medium (load discipline matters) |
214: 
215: **Out-of-scope moves** (these are not refactor; route elsewhere):
216: - Adding new behavior or capability → `skill-creator-advance`
217: - Changing input/output format → `skill-creator-advance`
218: - Different prompt phrasing aimed at *different* output → `skill-tuning`
219: - Removing a fallback / edge case handler → `skill-creator-advance`
220: 
221: ## Tier Cascade (when Q1's ensemble disagrees)
222: 
223: The multi-judge ensemble in Q1 might return mixed signals. The Tier
224: cascade governs how to handle disagreement:
225: 
226: | Tier | Trigger | Action |
227: |---|---|---|
228: | **Tier 2 override (first)** | Specific behavior dissent from any `not_equivalent` judge | `FLAG_TIER_2`; human review before count-based decisions |
229: | **Tier 1** | 3 `equivalent` | `PASS_HIGH`; auto pass |
230: | **Tier 1, moderate** | 2 `equivalent` + 1 `uncertain` | `PASS_MODERATE`; report confidence, may proceed |
231: | **Tier 2** | 2 `equivalent` + 1 `not_equivalent` | `FLAG_TIER_2`; hold for human review |
232: | **Reject** | ≤1 `equivalent`, after the dissent override | `FAIL`; reject |
233: | **Tier 3** | Human review finds the question is which output is better | Hand off to `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning` |
234: 
235: Tier 3 is the safety valve: when "is the output the same?" turns
236: into "which output is better?", we've left refactor territory.
237: 
238: ## Red Flags — STOP
239: 
240: | Flag | Why STOP |
241: |---|---|
242: | User wants the output to *improve*, not preserve | This is `skill-tuning` territory |
243: | Refactor adds new sections that change behavior | Two Hats violation — feature smuggled into refactor |
244: | Q1 says equivalent but token reduction <5% | The refactor cost exceeds the gain; reject |
245: | Q1 returns `FLAG_TIER_2` and you want to "majority-rules" anyway | Hold for human review; `PASS_MODERATE` remains a valid pass |
246: | Skill has no `test-prompts.json` and user is unwilling to write any | The gate cannot run; revert to `skill-creator-advance` |
247: | Entrypoint mode would touch `SKILL.md` and bundled files in one round | Split the round; package mode has the narrow directly-supporting-resource exception in §Round scope |
248: 
249: ## Rationalization Prevention
250: 
251: | Excuse | Reality |
252: |---|---|
253: | "It's just a small reorg." | Small reorgs accumulate. Run the gate anyway. |
254: | "Output is *probably* the same." | "Probably" isn't a verdict. Run the equivalence check. |
255: | "Token reduction is only 7% but it reads better." | "Reads better" is a `skill-tuning` claim, not a refactor claim. |
256: | "I'll skip the baseline this once." | Without baseline, Q1 cannot run. The skill self-aborts. |
257: | "The judge is wrong, my refactor is fine." | If you're overruling 2/3 judges, you're betting the user prefers your taste over consensus. Don't. |
258: 
259: ## Composes With
260: 
261: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-creator-advance`** — when refactor reveals a
262:   structural problem that needs redesign (not equivalence-preserving
263:   cleanup), hand off here.
264: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning`** — when the
265:   question turns from "are the outputs equivalent" to "which output
266:   is better", hand off here.
267: - **`skill-dev-toolkit:skill-judge`** — optional advisory check before /
268:   after refactor; if score drops by ≥1σ across rounds while
269:   equivalence keeps passing, this is a signal of subtle taste-drift
270:   that the equivalence check missed.
271: - **Refactoring discipline** — philosophical anchor: Fowler's Two Hats
272:   (separate behavior-preserving refactor from feature change). This skill
273:   is fully self-contained — no cross-plugin dependency.
274: 
275: ## Worked Example — token bloat in skill-creator-advance
276: 
277: This extraction illustrates why entrypoint shrinkage alone cannot pass Q2.
278: 
279: **Input**: User says "skill-creator-advance is at 5627 words, way
280: over the soft cap. Let's refactor."
281: 
282: **Phase 0 (baseline)**:
283: - Capture `test-prompts.json` if absent — 3 prompts covering
284:   creation / improvement / description optimization
285: - Run baseline: each prompt × current skill → outputs
286: - Entrypoint count: 5627 words; capture whole-package baseline under package mode
287: 
288: **Phase 1 (Q1+Q2+Q3)**:
289: - Candidate move: extract "Description Optimization" section (§
290:   lines 430-501, ~700 words) to `references/description-optimization.md`
291: - Re-run test prompts on candidate
292: - Q1 ensemble: 3/3 say outputs equivalent (the description-opt
293:   use case still works because the section is loaded on demand
294:   via reference)
295: - Q2: entrypoint 5627 → 4927 words = 12.4% smaller, but the ~700 words
296:   remain in the new reference. Whole-package words stay the same or increase
297:   with the pointer; moving prose alone earns no reduction.
298: - Q3: name and existing dependencies preserved; the new bundled reference
299:   is required for equivalent extraction (allowed in package mode) ✓
300: 
301: **Verdict**: REJECT. Discard this candidate: whole-package Q2 fails even
302: though Q1 and Q3 pass. A later candidate must remove actual duplication
303: and pass Q1/Q2/Q3 on the whole package.
304: 
305: **Phase 2 (next round)**:
306: - Look for actual duplication to compress, rather than counting relocated words
307: - Capture and verify the next isolated candidate independently
308: 
309: ## Worked Example — refactor that should be REJECT
310: 
311: **Input**: User says "let me try this rewording — it's clearer".
312: 
313: **Q1 result**: 2/3 judges say outputs equivalent; 1 says the
314: candidate's output is "less specific about edge cases". Tier 2.
315: - This is the warning sign — judge disagreement on a rewording move.
316: 
317: **Q2 result**: 5% reduction.
318: 
319: **Q3 result**: clean.
320: 
321: **Verdict**: HOLD (`FLAG_TIER_2`) → user examines the dissenting judge's
322: reasoning. Turns out the "clearer" rewording dropped a phrase that
323: nudged Claude to handle a specific edge case. The candidate is
324: **not actually equivalent** — it's a subtle behavior change masked
325: as refactor.
326: 
327: → Reject this round; do not commit.
328: 
329: This is what the multi-judge ensemble is for: catching subtle
330: behavior changes that single-judge or pure structural check would
331: miss.
332: 
333: ## When To Apply
334: 
335: **Primary triggers**:
336: - "Shorten this skill" / "reduce token count" / "縮減 SKILL.md"
337: - "Skill is too long; tidy it up without changing behavior"
338: - "Refactor SKILL.md keeping outputs the same"
339: - "リファクタ skill / トークン削減"
340: - "整理 skill 結構"
341: 
342: **Shape**: A single existing skill with ≥3 documented use cases
343: (needed for `test-prompts.json`). Token bloat or structural mess is
344: the symptom; output behavior is the invariant.
345: 
346: **Not-triggers** — do NOT invoke for:
347: 
348: - **Skill output is bad / wrong** → use `skill-tuning` (taste
349:   improvement, not equivalence-preserving)
350: - **Want to add a phase / change agent / restructure workflow** →
351:   use `skill-creator-advance` (structural rewrite)
352: - **Creating a new skill** → use `skill-creator-advance`
353: - **Single-line cosmetic edits** — just edit directly; the gate
354:   cost exceeds the change cost
355: - **Skill has no `test-prompts.json` and user won't write one** —
356:   the gate cannot run; either provide test prompts or use
357:   `skill-creator-advance` to redesign with proper test infra
358: - **Skill output is creative / non-deterministic** (writing style,
359:   prose, design feel) — equivalence check unreliable; use
360:   `skill-tuning`
361: 
362: ## Bundled Resources
363: 
364: This skill is fully self-contained at runtime. References:
365: 
366: - `references/equivalence-check-protocol.md` — Q1's structural +
367:   semantic check details
368: - `references/multi-judge-ensemble.md` — how to spawn 3 independent
369:   judges with varied framing; consensus rules
370: - `references/refactor-moves-catalog.md` — full catalog of
371:   refactor-hat-safe moves with examples
372: - `references/golden-anchor-protocol.md` — golden anchor schema and
373:   curation policy (shared convention with `skill-tuning`; same-PR
374:   drift rule)
375: - `references/test-prompts-schema.md` — `test-prompts.json` schema
376:   (shared convention with `skill-tuning`; same-PR drift rule)
377: - `references/constitution-schema.md` — `constitution.md` schema
378:   used as Q3 invariant input (shared convention with `skill-tuning`;
379:   same-PR drift rule)
380: 
381: Scripts:
382: 
383: - `scripts/equivalence_check.py` — orchestrates Q1's structural
384:   comparison
385: - `scripts/multi_judge.py` — runs 3-judge ensemble, returns consensus
386: - `scripts/golden_compare.py` — compares candidate output to a
387:   golden reference (optional `PASS_MODERATE` supplement; comparison only,
388:   cannot clear `FLAG_TIER_2` or replace human review)
389: 
390: For a bundled-resource refactor only, read
391: `references/package-resource-mode.md`. It supplies the immutable-baseline,
392: whole-package accounting, and layered evidence protocol; entrypoint-only
393: requests do not load it.
394: 
395: Optional golden check: for `PASS_HIGH` or `PASS_MODERATE`, if golden anchors exist
396: in `<skill>/golden/`, a selected comparison can reject an anchor regression.
397: A positive result cannot clear `FLAG_TIER_2` or replace human review;
398: existing Q1/Q2/Q3 rules and cumulative-drift safeguards still apply.
399: See `references/golden-anchor-protocol.md`.
400: 
401: ## Bottom Line
402: 
403: ```
404: Output preserved. Tokens shrunk. Invariants intact. Otherwise discard the candidate.
405: Refactor is structure, not feature. Prove equivalence; don't promise it.
406: ```

FILE skill-refactor/references/ablation-mode.md
1: # Ablation Mode — section-splitter + worked example
2: 
3: Detail behind `SKILL.md` §Ablation mode. Use this to find which sections of a
4: skill add words without adding behavioral capability, **without** needing a
5: control group (ancestor / sibling skill) to compare against.
6: 
7: ## Taxonomy-guided candidate pre-pass
8: 
9: Leave-one-out is expensive per section — `(1 + N_sections) × N_relevant_prompts`
10: runs plus judges (see Cost below). **Before** running it, do a cheap read-through
11: pass: scan each section of the target skill against a bloat taxonomy and count
12: hits. This produces no verdicts by itself — it only **orders** which sections get
13: the expensive ablation treatment first, so the budget lands on likely-bloat
14: sections instead of being spent in file order.
15: 
16: Taxonomy (five categories to scan for, per section):
17: 
18: - **No-op sentences** — text that asserts nothing checkable; deleting it changes
19:   no behavior (e.g. "this is an important step" with no test attached).
20: - **Sediment** — leftovers of a superseded revision that no longer binds anything
21:   current (an old workflow's vocabulary, a caveat for a mechanism since removed).
22: - **Negative instructions** — "don't do X" phrasing ("don't think of an elephant" —
23:   naming the forbidden move can itself trigger it); prefer restating the rule as
24:   the positive behavior wanted instead of the thing to avoid.
25: - **Premature completion claims** — text that declares success or done-ness the
26:   workflow hasn't actually earned yet (asserting an outcome before the step that
27:   produces it).
28: - **Duplication with a bundled reference** — body prose restates content a
29:   `references/*.md` file already owns, instead of pointing to it.
30: 
31: Rank sections by taxonomy-hit count (most hits first) and run the leave-one-out
32: procedure below in that descending-suspicion order.
33: 
34: **This pre-pass orders candidates only.** It does not cut anything, and it does
35: not change the gate: every actual cut — regardless of how it was surfaced —
36: still passes the Q1/Q2/Q3 gate before landing. The "candidate-finder, not
37: auto-deleter" discipline (`SKILL.md` §Ablation mode) is unchanged; the pre-pass
38: is just a cheaper way to decide which candidate to check first.
39: 
40: Taxonomy adapted from Matt Pocock's `mattpocock/skills` (`writing-great-skills`,
41: MIT) / aihero.dev.
42: 
43: ## Section splitter (leave-one-out)
44: 
45: Split the target SKILL.md into top-level `## ` sections (keep the frontmatter +
46: any pre-header preamble in every variant). For each section, emit a variant that
47: is the full skill **minus that one section**. Pseudocode:
48: 
49: ```python
50: # read SKILL.md → keep frontmatter (head) + split body on top-level "## " headers
51: # for each section i: variant_i = head + body with section i removed
52: # write each variant to /tmp/<skill>-abl/abl-<i>-<slug>.md
53: ```
54: 
55: Run the same `test-prompts.json` (the gate's behavioral eval) through the FULL
56: skill and through each variant; a judge ensemble compares full-vs-ablated per
57: section. **Map each section to the prompt(s) that exercise its purpose** — a
58: section whose trigger no prompt hits will read as bloat regardless (the coverage
59: caveat). For navigational sections, judge by hand.
60: 
61: ## Cost
62: 
63: Roughly `(1 + N_sections) × N_relevant_prompts` skill runs, plus 3 judges
64: for each full-vs-ablated prompt comparison, using Q1's ensemble. Bound it:
65: feed each section only the prompt(s) that exercise it (not the full cross product).
66: 
67: ## Verdict mapping
68: 
69: | Ensemble on a section | Meaning | Action |
70: |---|---|---|
71: | `PASS_HIGH` (3 equivalent) or `PASS_MODERATE` (2 equivalent + 1 uncertain) | BLOAT candidate; report confidence | compress or cut only after Q1/Q2/Q3 |
72: | Specific behavior dissent, before count rules | `FLAG_TIER_2`; possible load-bearing behavior | hold; inspect the cited loss |
73: | 2 equivalent + 1 not_equivalent | `FLAG_TIER_2` | investigate by hand / add a sharper test prompt |
74: | ≤1 equivalent after dissent override | `FAIL`; removal fails equivalence | keep pending investigation |
75: | Disagreement across **two related sections** | possible redundancy trap | investigate overlap; test a merged candidate through Q1/Q2/Q3 rather than delete both |
76: 
77: ## Worked example — verification-before-completion (2026-05-30 prototype)
78: 
79: This historical two-judge prototype records its original votes and call count;
80: current runs use the three-judge Q1 policy above. It ran in ~11 subagent calls.
81: 
82: 7 sections, 2 prompts (P1 = "ran the test FILE directly, good to merge?" → correct
83: = refuse + demand package-level run; P2 = "bumped version + README, done without
84: tests?" → correct = doc/config exemption). 2-judge ensemble:
85: 
86: | Section removed | Verdict |
87: |---|---|
88: | When NOT to use | **LOAD-BEARING** (2/2) — removal made the agent demand `npm test` on a doc change (broke the exemption) |
89: | Process | BLOAT (2/2) — agent reconstructed the procedure from the HARD-GATE alone |
90: | Cross-skill contract | BLOAT (2/2) — integration table never fires on a direct user prompt |
91: | What this skill does NOT do | BLOAT (2/2) — scope prose; behavior survives |
92: | See also | BLOAT (2/2) — link list; zero enforcement |
93: | The HARD-GATE | SPLIT — bloat per judge A *because Red Flags covers it* |
94: | Red Flags | SPLIT — bloat per judge B *because the HARD-GATE covers it* |
95: 
96: **Reading:** the positive control fired (When-NOT-to-use is load-bearing, caught
97: by both judges). The HARD-GATE / Red-Flags SPLIT is the redundancy trap — they
98: overlap (both encode "refuse unverified done-claims, demand package-level run"),
99: so leave-one-out shows each as removable, but you can't remove both → **merge
100: them**. Refactor targets produced with no control group: merge HARD-GATE+Red-Flags,
101: compress Process (drop the detection-signals list that duplicates
102: `test-invocation-by-stack.md`), trim Cross-skill / What-NOT / See-also.
103: 
104: ## Sources
105: 
106: - ablation studies on prompt components are non-monotonic — generic additions can
107:   *degrade* task accuracy; validate each component against a task eval, not intuition
108:   (arXiv:2601.22025, "When Better Prompts Hurt").
109: - context rot: surplus input tokens measurably degrade output quality, complex tasks
110:   worst (Chroma 2025; LongLLMLingua arXiv:2310.06839 — 4× fewer tokens, +17% accuracy).
111:   So a confirmed-bloat cut is often net-positive, not neutral.

FILE skill-refactor/references/constitution-schema.md
1: # Constitution Schema
2: 
3: > **Shared convention — canonical SoT.** This file is bundled in
4: > both `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-refactor/references/` and
5: > `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning/references/` as functional copies.
6: > This location is the canonical SoT for evolution; same-PR drift
7: > rule mirrors edits to the `skill-tuning` copy.
8: 
9: Schema for `constitution.md` — a per-skill MUST / MUST NOT
10: specification used as invariant input by `skill-refactor` Q3 and as
11: judging criteria by `skill-tuning`.
12: 
13: ## Purpose
14: 
15: A skill's constitution is the **explicit, non-negotiable
16: specification of what the skill must / must not do**. It captures
17: the load-bearing behavioral guarantees that any refactor or
18: variant must preserve.
19: 
20: Compare to:
21: 
22: - `description` (in frontmatter): triggers and 1-paragraph summary
23: - `SKILL.md body`: the prose instruction set
24: - `test-prompts.json`: input set for evaluation
25: - `golden/`: human-curated reference outputs
26: 
27: The constitution sits **above** all of these — it's the contract
28: the skill commits to, the prose / structure / examples are
29: *implementations of the contract*.
30: 
31: ## File location
32: 
33: ```
34: <skill-dir>/constitution.md
35: ```
36: 
37: Same level as `SKILL.md`. Optional file — many skills don't need a
38: formal constitution. When present, it's authoritative for behavior
39: guarantees.
40: 
41: ## Schema
42: 
43: ```markdown
44: # Constitution: <skill-name>
45: 
46: **Schema version**: 1
47: **Authored**: YYYY-MM-DD
48: **Last reviewed**: YYYY-MM-DD
49: **Authored by**: <human>
50: 
51: ## MUST
52: 
53: The skill MUST:
54: 
55: 1. <observable behavior 1>
56: 2. <observable behavior 2>
57: 3. ...
58: 
59: ## MUST NOT
60: 
61: The skill MUST NOT:
62: 
63: 1. <prohibited behavior 1>
64: 2. <prohibited behavior 2>
65: 3. ...
66: 
67: ## SHOULD (advisory)
68: 
69: The skill SHOULD (but failures are not constitution violations):
70: 
71: 1. <preferred behavior 1>
72: 2. ...
73: 
74: ## Operational definitions
75: 
76: <glossary of terms used in MUST / MUST NOT to disambiguate>
77: ```
78: 
79: ## Writing MUST clauses
80: 
81: Each MUST is a **single observable behavior**, expressed as a
82: testable statement.
83: 
84: Good MUST examples:
85: - "MUST ask the user to confirm before deleting any file"
86: - "MUST cite a primary source for any factual claim"
87: - "MUST produce output in markdown format"
88: - "MUST handle empty input by asking for clarification, not by
89:   returning silently"
90: 
91: Bad MUST examples:
92: - "MUST be high quality" (untestable, taste)
93: - "MUST be helpful" (untestable)
94: - "MUST handle all edge cases" (over-broad)
95: - "MUST follow best practices" (defers definition)
96: 
97: Each MUST should be checkable from a single test prompt + output
98: pair. If you can't write a single yes/no test for a MUST clause,
99: it's too vague.
100: 
101: ## Writing MUST NOT clauses
102: 
103: Mirror to MUST. Each MUST NOT is an explicit prohibition with a
104: checkable signal.
105: 
106: Good MUST NOT:
107: - "MUST NOT execute commands without showing them to the user first"
108: - "MUST NOT produce more than 500 words of output without
109:   user confirmation"
110: - "MUST NOT call external APIs without explicit user opt-in"
111: 
112: Bad MUST NOT:
113: - "MUST NOT be wrong" (untestable)
114: - "MUST NOT bother the user" (subjective)
115: 
116: ## SHOULD clauses
117: 
118: SHOULD is for preferences that aren't constitution-grade —
119: violation is acceptable but should be defended. Goes here, not in
120: MUST, when:
121: 
122: - The behavior is preferred but skill remains useful without it
123: - Compliance can vary by user / context
124: - Failure is recoverable / non-load-bearing
125: 
126: Example:
127: - "SHOULD respond in the user's language when detectable"
128: - "SHOULD include a one-line rationale with each verdict"
129: 
130: ## Operational definitions
131: 
132: When MUST / MUST NOT clauses use domain-specific terms, define
133: them in this section to avoid ambiguity.
134: 
135: Example (for a citation-checking skill):
136: - **Primary source**: original publication (book, paper, official
137:   document); not a blog summary or aggregator
138: - **Citation**: author + year + work title + (where applicable)
139:   page / chapter / URL
140: 
141: Without operational definitions, MUST clauses are interpretation-
142: dependent and can't ground objective evaluation.
143: 
144: ## How `skill-refactor` uses constitution
145: 
146: In Q3 (invariant preservation):
147: 
148: ```
149: For each MUST clause in constitution.md:
150:   Run candidate skill on a representative test prompt
151:   Check whether the MUST behavior holds
152:   
153: For each MUST NOT clause:
154:   Check whether the prohibition is honored
155: 
156: If any MUST violated or any MUST NOT triggered → REJECT round
157: ```
158: 
159: A refactor that breaks a MUST is **not a refactor** — it's a
160: behavior change. Constitutional check is what catches "smuggled
161: feature work".
162: 
163: ## How `skill-tuning` uses constitution
164: 
165: In its variant evaluation:
166: 
167: ```
168: For each variant output:
169:   Score: how well does this output honor the constitution?
170:   
171: Constitutional honoring is the floor; taste is the ceiling.
172: A variant that violates a MUST is rejected even if user
173: "prefers" the output — taste does not override constitution.
174: ```
175: 
176: ## When constitution is unnecessary
177: 
178: Many skills don't need a formal constitution. Indicators:
179: 
180: - Skill is a deterministic utility (no real interpretive freedom)
181: - Skill's behavior is fully captured by SKILL.md prose with no
182:   load-bearing nuance
183: - No taste-sensitive dimensions (constitution is most valuable
184:   exactly where taste enters)
185: - Skill is < 1 year old and behavior is stable — write
186:   constitution later if needed
187: 
188: For such skills, skip the constitution.md file. SKILL.md alone is
189: the spec.
190: 
191: ## When constitution is essential
192: 
193: Constitution is essential when:
194: 
195: - Skill produces output where "right" varies with use case
196: - Skill has ≥1 documented behavior that contributors might
197:   unintentionally weaken (e.g., "always cite source" is a
198:   constitution-grade rule because it's easy to soften by accident)
199: - Skill is iterated frequently — constitution prevents drift
200: - Multiple contributors edit the skill — constitution is the
201:   shared contract
202: 
203: ## Curation policy
204: 
205: ### Authoring
206: 
207: Constitution is **always human-authored**. Like goldens, an
208: LLM-generated constitution defeats the purpose — the value is
209: specifically that a human committed to these guarantees.
210: 
211: ### Review
212: 
213: Re-review every 6 months or when:
214: 
215: - A refactor / tuning round reveals a MUST that's actually
216:   weaker than written
217: - User feedback identifies a behavior the skill should commit
218:   to that's not yet in MUST
219: - A MUST has been weakened in practice — either restore it or
220:   formally relax it (and document why)
221: 
222: ### Versioning
223: 
224: Constitution changes are major events. Update `Last reviewed`,
225: note the change reason in commit message, and ideally bump the
226: target skill's version (minor or major depending on whether the
227: MUST changed in a backward-compatible way).
228: 
229: ## Anti-patterns
230: 
231: | Anti-pattern | Why bad |
232: |---|---|
233: | Vague MUST ("be helpful") | Untestable; can't ground evaluation |
234: | Constitution = SKILL.md restated | Adds no information; should be the *contract above* the prose |
235: | Constitution longer than SKILL.md | Probably over-specified; trim to essentials |
236: | LLM-generated constitution | Defeats the human-anchor purpose |
237: | MUST that's actually a SHOULD | False rigidity; either lift compliance or move to SHOULD |
238: | Constitution never re-reviewed | Drifts; becomes ceremonial |
239: 
240: ## Lifecycle
241: 
242: | Event | Action |
243: |---|---|
244: | Skill created | Author writes initial constitution if behavior is taste-sensitive or load-bearing |
245: | Skill refactored | No constitution change (refactor preserves behavior) |
246: | Skill tasted | If A/B reveals an implicit constraint, formalize as MUST |
247: | Skill rewritten | Re-author constitution if behavior changed |
248: | 6 months pass | Recommend re-review |

FILE skill-refactor/references/equivalence-check-protocol.md
1: # Equivalence Check Protocol
2: 
3: How `skill-refactor` Q1 verifies that a candidate refactored
4: SKILL.md produces output equivalent to the baseline.
5: 
6: ## Two-layer check
7: 
8: ### Layer 1: Structural comparison (deterministic)
9: 
10: Run by `scripts/equivalence_check.py`. Cheap, fast, catches gross
11: divergence.
12: 
13: For each test prompt's `(baseline_output, candidate_output)` pair:
14: 
15: | Check | Pass criterion | Failure example |
16: |---|---|---|
17: | Output type match | Same artifact types produced (file, JSON, prose, code) | Baseline produced `report.md`, candidate produced JSON |
18: | Section structure | Same set of `## ` heading texts (order may vary) | Candidate dropped a "Summary" section |
19: | File paths | Same set of file paths created / referenced | Candidate wrote to a different path |
20: | Tool calls | Identical tool-name sequences, or one insertion/deletion preserving remaining order and identical tool vocabulary | Candidate reversed `Read`, `Write` |
21: | Token count | Within ±20% of baseline | Output dropped 60% — likely missing content |
22: 
23: **Pass rule**: all 5 checks pass. Layer 1 failure → REJECT
24: immediately, do not proceed to Layer 2.
25: 
26: Tool traces of the same length must match exactly, including repeated calls.
27: When counts differ by one, deleting one call from the longer trace must
28: produce the shorter trace. Both traces must still use the same set of tool
29: names; adding or removing the only occurrence of a tool fails. Counts that
30: differ by more than one fail. When neither output provides a tool trace,
31: the tool-call check is skipped as before.
32: 
33: ### Layer 2: LLM-judge ensemble (semantic)
34: 
35: If Layer 1 passes, run 3 independent LLM judges (see
36: `multi-judge-ensemble.md`). Each judge receives:
37: 
38: - The user prompt that produced both outputs
39: - Both outputs labeled `Output_A` / `Output_B` (random which is
40:   baseline)
41: - Asked: "Are A and B semantically equivalent? Same information,
42:   same recommendations, same actions?"
43: - Returns: `equivalent` / `not_equivalent` / `uncertain` + 1-2
44:   sentence reason
45: 
46: **Consensus rules**:
47: 
48: | Result | Verdict |
49: |---|---|
50: | 3/3 `equivalent` | PASS (high confidence) |
51: | 2/3 `equivalent`, 1 `uncertain` | PASS (moderate confidence) |
52: | 2/3 `equivalent`, 1 `not_equivalent` | FLAG → Tier 2 (escalate to human) |
53: | 1/3 `equivalent` (or worse) | FAIL → REJECT round |
54: | Any `not_equivalent` reason cites specific behavior change | FLAG → Tier 2 even if 2/3 said equivalent |
55: 
56: **Important rule**: a single dissenting judge that points to a
57: *specific behavior change* outranks numeric majority. The dissent
58: is a signal that the refactor smuggled feature work; investigate
59: before committing.
60: 
61: ## What "equivalent" means
62: 
63: Equivalent means **the user gets the same value from the output**:
64: 
65: - Same recommendations / answers / verdicts
66: - Same files / artifacts created (paths and contents semantically
67:   matching)
68: - Same edge cases handled
69: - Same warnings / caveats raised
70: - Same level of detail (within reason; some compression OK if no
71:   load-bearing detail dropped)
72: 
73: Equivalent does **not** require:
74: 
75: - Identical phrasing
76: - Identical word counts
77: - Identical sentence ordering
78: - Same tone (as long as content is preserved)
79: 
80: The line is: "could the user complete the same task with this
81: output as with the baseline?" If yes → equivalent. If no → not.
82: 
83: ## What invalidates a "pass" verdict
84: 
85: Even if both layers pass, mark as suspicious if:
86: 
87: - Layer 1 pass but Layer 2 has any `uncertain` votes → moderate
88:   confidence at best
89: - Token count delta is suspiciously large (>30% reduction in
90:   output) → may indicate dropped content the structural check
91:   didn't catch
92: - Edge-case test prompts (intentionally tricky inputs) don't
93:   match — happy-path equivalence is necessary but not sufficient
94: 
95: ## How to design test prompts for equivalence checking
96: 
97: Per `test-prompts-schema.md`, each skill should have ≥3 prompts:
98: 
99: 1. **Happy path** — most common use case
100: 2. **Edge case** — tricky input that exercises a specific instruction
101: 3. **Stress prompt** — input deliberately incomplete or ambiguous
102:    (tests the skill's intake / clarification behavior)
103: 
104: Refactor that passes happy path but fails edge case is a **subtle
105: regression**; the equivalence check needs to cover both.
106: 
107: ## Failure modes to avoid
108: 
109: | Failure mode | What goes wrong | Mitigation |
110: |---|---|---|
111: | **Single judge bias** | One judge consistently says equivalent (or not) regardless | 3-judge ensemble + varied prompt framing |
112: | **Verbosity bias** | Judge prefers longer output → marks shorter candidate as "less complete" | Layer 1 token-count rule prevents this from auto-rejecting; Layer 2 sees both with neutral framing |
113: | **Position bias** | Judge prefers `Output_A` over `Output_B` | Random labeling in Layer 2 (sometimes baseline = A, sometimes = B) |
114: | **Self-preference** | Judge prefers output it would have produced | Use different prompt frames per judge |
115: | **Insensitivity to subtle change** | Judges miss a small but load-bearing word change | Fall back to `skill-tuning` if you suspect this; refactor is the wrong tool for subtle taste |
116: 
117: ## When this protocol is the wrong fit
118: 
119: If the target skill produces non-deterministic / creative output
120: (writing tone, design choices, persuasive copy), Layer 2's judge
121: becomes unreliable — taste enters. The skill self-aborts and
122: recommends `skill-tuning` instead. This is documented in
123: SKILL.md §Not-triggers.

FILE skill-refactor/references/golden-anchor-protocol.md
1: # Golden Anchor Protocol
2: 
3: > **Shared convention — canonical SoT.** This file is bundled in
4: > both `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-refactor/references/` and
5: > `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning/references/` as functional copies.
6: > This location is the canonical SoT for the convention's
7: > evolution; the `skill-tuning` copy is updated to match in the
8: > same PR (drift rule). Runtime self-containment preserved on
9: > each side.
10: 
11: How to maintain a `golden/` directory of curated reference outputs
12: for a skill, used as a stable evaluation anchor for refactor and
13: tuning work.
14: 
15: ## What goldens are
16: 
17: Golden anchors are **human-curated examples of "what good output
18: looks like"** for a given skill. Each is a `(prompt, output)` pair
19: that the curator has reviewed and judged "this is the kind of
20: output we want this skill to produce".
21: 
22: Goldens differ from `test-prompts.json`:
23: 
24: | | `test-prompts.json` | `golden/` |
25: |---|---|---|
26: | Purpose | Run-on-current-skill input | Reference output to compare against |
27: | Authored by | Often LLM-drafted, user-confirmed | Always human-curated |
28: | What it tests | "Skill produces *some* output" | "Skill produces *this kind* of output" |
29: | Used by | Equivalence check (Q1) | Optional anchor comparison (PASS_MODERATE supplement, taste check) |
30: 
31: ## When goldens matter
32: 
33: Goldens are **optional but high-value** for skills where:
34: 
35: - Output has **taste-sensitive dimensions** (writing style, tone,
36:   voice, design feel)
37: - Output structure is conventional but the *quality* of fill-in is
38:   what matters (e.g., a status report has fixed sections, but the
39:   content quality varies)
40: - Equivalence check alone is insufficient — two outputs can be
41:   "equivalent" in content but one is clearly better
42: 
43: For purely mechanical skills (file transforms, fixed-format
44: generators), goldens are overkill — equivalence check suffices.
45: 
46: ## Directory layout
47: 
48: ```
49: <skill>/golden/
50: ├── README.md                    ← curation policy, when last reviewed, who authored
51: ├── golden-1.md                  ← (prompt, output) pair
52: ├── golden-2.md
53: └── ...
54: ```
55: 
56: Each golden file:
57: 
58: ```markdown
59: ---
60: id: golden-1
61: authored_by: <human>
62: authored_date: YYYY-MM-DD
63: last_reviewed: YYYY-MM-DD
64: prompt_pattern: "user request type that should trigger this anchor"
65: quality_dimensions:
66:   - tone: friendly-professional
67:   - length: concise (< 200 words)
68:   - structure: 3-section
69: ---
70: 
71: # Prompt
72: 
73: <the user prompt>
74: 
75: # Anchor Output
76: 
77: <the golden output — what the skill should produce>
78: 
79: # Why This Is Good
80: 
81: <1-2 paragraphs from the curator explaining what makes this
82: output good; specifically the dimensions called out in
83: quality_dimensions>
84: ```
85: 
86: ## Curation policy
87: 
88: ### How many goldens per skill
89: 
90: - **Minimum**: 3 for skills where goldens apply
91: - **Typical**: 5-10 covering core use cases
92: - **Maximum useful**: ~15; beyond that, marginal value drops
93: 
94: ### Who curates
95: 
96: Goldens are **human work**, not AI-generated. The curator should:
97: 
98: - Be familiar with the skill's intended use
99: - Have judgment about what "good output" means in this context
100: - Be the same person across goldens for one skill (consistency)
101: - Document quality dimensions in frontmatter so other readers
102:   understand the criteria
103: 
104: ### Curation process
105: 
106: 1. Pick a representative prompt for the skill
107: 2. Run the skill on it (current best version)
108: 3. Either:
109:    - Accept the output as-is if it's truly good
110:    - Edit the output to be the version that demonstrates "good"
111:    - Write the desired output from scratch
112: 4. Document in "Why This Is Good" what makes it good
113: 5. Save to `golden/golden-N.md`
114: 6. Re-review every 6 months or when the skill significantly evolves
115: 
116: ### Review trigger
117: 
118: Goldens become stale. The curator should re-review:
119: 
120: - Every 6 months as a default rhythm
121: - When the skill has had a major rewrite (post-skill-creator-advance)
122: - When user feedback on the skill changes
123: - When a refactor or tuning round reveals goldens no longer
124:   reflect current judgment
125: 
126: Stale goldens are worse than no goldens — they create false
127: anchoring.
128: 
129: ## How `skill-refactor` uses goldens
130: 
131: Q1's existing vote outcome remains authoritative:
132: 
133: - 2 `equivalent` + 1 `uncertain`, without specific behavior dissent,
134:   yields `PASS_MODERATE` and may proceed under the existing Q1/Q2/Q3 rules.
135: - 2 `equivalent` + 1 `not_equivalent`, or specific behavior dissent,
136:   yields `FLAG_TIER_2`: hold for human review. A positive anchor comparison
137:   cannot clear this flag or replace human review.
138: 
139: Golden comparison is an **optional supplement** to `PASS_MODERATE`, not
140: a required escalation; `PASS_HIGH` candidates may also use it. If selected,
141: an anchor regression can reject the candidate; a positive result supplies
142: comparison evidence only.
143: 
144: ```
145: If Q1 = FLAG_TIER_2:
146:   HOLD for human review, regardless of anchor similarity
147: Else if Q1 in {PASS_HIGH, PASS_MODERATE} and anchors exist and check is selected:
148:   For each test prompt with matching anchors:
149:     compare candidate and baseline outputs to the relevant golden anchors
150: 
151:     If candidate's similarity to relevant goldens < baseline's:
152:       REJECT candidate — moved away from anchored quality
153:     Else:
154:       Record positive anchor evidence — existing Q1/Q2/Q3 rules still apply
155: ```
156: 
157: The comparison target is human-fixed, but textual similarity does not
158: establish semantic equivalence or authorize adoption. This optional check
159: does not alter the separate cumulative-drift safeguard: after 3 consecutive
160: moderate-confidence PROCEEDs, flag the cumulative diff for human review.
161: 
162: ## How `skill-tuning` uses goldens
163: 
164: In `skill-tuning` (per its protocol), goldens serve as the
165: ground-truth direction for variant exploration:
166: 
167: ```
168: For each generated variant:
169:   similarity_to_golden = compare(variant_output, nearest_golden)
170:   
171: Variants that move away from golden = bad
172: Variants that move toward golden = good
173: Human selection still happens, but golden gives a baseline
174: "closer / further" signal.
175: ```
176: 
177: ## Drift management
178: 
179: The `golden/` directory is human-managed. Drift rules:
180: 
181: - Don't auto-edit goldens (this defeats the human-anchored purpose)
182: - Don't generate new goldens from skill outputs without human review
183: - When skill behavior must change (post-rewrite via
184:   skill-creator-advance), update goldens explicitly in the same PR
185:   as the skill change — don't leave dangling pre-rewrite goldens
186: 
187: ## Anti-patterns
188: 
189: | Anti-pattern | Why it breaks goldens |
190: |---|---|
191: | AI-generated goldens | Defeats the purpose; goldens *are* the human anchor |
192: | Goldens that look identical to current skill output | Adds no information; just baseline-locks |
193: | Goldens that are unrealistic / aspirational | Pushes skill in directions it can't reach |
194: | One golden per skill | Insufficient variance; can't represent quality range |
195: | Stale goldens (>1 year old, never re-reviewed) | False anchor; better to have no golden than wrong golden |
196: | Goldens that don't explain "Why This Is Good" | Can't be applied; reader doesn't know what dimensions matter |
197: 
198: ## Skip if not applicable
199: 
200: Many skills don't need goldens. Indicators:
201: 
202: - Output is purely deterministic (file transforms, JSON
203:   generation)
204: - "Good output" is binary (correct / incorrect, not better / worse)
205: - Skill is a one-step utility (no taste dimension)
206: 
207: For such skills, `skill-refactor` Q1's equivalence check + Q2's
208: token reduction + Q3's invariant check is sufficient. Goldens are
209: overkill.
210: 
211: This protocol applies only when goldens are warranted. Document
212: the decision in the skill's NOTICE: "Goldens not applicable
213: because <reason>".

FILE skill-refactor/references/multi-judge-ensemble.md
1: # Multi-Judge Ensemble
2: 
3: How to spawn 3 independent LLM judges for `skill-refactor` Q1's
4: Layer 2 semantic check.
5: 
6: ## Why ensemble
7: 
8: Single LLM-as-judge has documented failure modes (verbosity bias,
9: position bias, self-preference, insensitivity to subtle change).
10: A 3-judge ensemble:
11: 
12: 1. **Reduces variance** — single judge's idiosyncratic verdict gets
13:    averaged out
14: 2. **Surfaces uncertainty** — disagreement among judges informs the
15:    consensus rules; escalate only `FLAG_TIER_2` outcomes to human review
16: 3. **Catches subtle behavior change** — a single dissenting judge
17:    pointing to a specific behavior diff outranks numeric majority
18: 
19: 3 is the minimum that allows majority vote with a tiebreaker; more
20: judges (5, 7) reduce variance further but cost more tokens. Default
21: to 3.
22: 
23: ## How to spawn
24: 
25: Each judge runs as a separate `Task` subagent with a different
26: prompt framing.
27: 
28: ### Judge 1: Functional equivalence (utility-oriented)
29: 
30: ```
31: You are evaluating whether two outputs serve the same user goal.
32: 
33: User's request: <prompt>
34: Output A: <output_a>
35: Output B: <output_b>
36: 
37: Question: Could the user accomplish the same task with Output A as
38: with Output B? Same recommendations, same actions, same artifacts?
39: 
40: Answer with one of:
41: - equivalent: yes, same value to user
42: - not_equivalent: no, A and B differ in load-bearing way
43: - uncertain: borderline; explain what's unclear
44: 
45: Then give a 1-2 sentence reason. If "not_equivalent", name the
46: specific behavior change.
47: ```
48: 
49: ### Judge 2: Information completeness (content-oriented)
50: 
51: ```
52: You are checking whether two outputs contain the same information.
53: 
54: User's request: <prompt>
55: Output A: <output_a>
56: Output B: <output_b>
57: 
58: Question: Does either output contain information the other lacks?
59: Edge cases, warnings, recommendations, examples? Ignore phrasing /
60: ordering / tone differences.
61: 
62: Answer with one of:
63: - equivalent: same information set
64: - not_equivalent: one contains load-bearing content the other lacks
65: - uncertain: surface differences but unclear if load-bearing
66: 
67: Then give a 1-2 sentence reason. If "not_equivalent", name the
68: specific content gap.
69: ```
70: 
71: ### Judge 3: Edge-case behavior (boundary-oriented)
72: 
73: ```
74: You are checking whether two outputs handle the same edge cases.
75: 
76: User's request: <prompt>
77: Output A: <output_a>
78: Output B: <output_b>
79: 
80: Question: For inputs near the boundary of the request — ambiguous
81: phrasing, missing information, unusual values — would A and B
82: guide the user the same way? Or does one skip checks the other
83: performs?
84: 
85: Answer with one of:
86: - equivalent: same boundary behavior
87: - not_equivalent: one handles a boundary case the other doesn't
88: - uncertain: boundaries unclear from the prompt
89: 
90: Then give a 1-2 sentence reason. If "not_equivalent", name the
91: specific boundary behavior diff.
92: ```
93: 
94: ## Why varied framing
95: 
96: Each frame tests a different equivalence aspect:
97: 
98: | Judge | Frame | What it catches |
99: |---|---|---|
100: | 1 (utility) | "same value to user" | Output structure preserved |
101: | 2 (content) | "same information set" | No content silently dropped |
102: | 3 (boundary) | "same edge-case handling" | No fallback / warning lost |
103: 
104: Without varied framing, all three judges would tend to align (LLM
105: self-preference). Varied framing forces them to look at different
106: dimensions.
107: 
108: ## Random output labeling (position bias mitigation)
109: 
110: For each judge call, **randomly assign baseline / candidate to A /
111: B**. Don't always put baseline as A. Some judges are biased toward
112: "first option" or "second option" regardless of content. Random
113: labeling averages this out across the ensemble.
114: 
115: Track the assignment in the call log so you can decode the result:
116: 
117: ```json
118: {
119:   "judge_id": 1,
120:   "frame": "utility",
121:   "a_is": "baseline",
122:   "b_is": "candidate",
123:   "verdict": "equivalent",
124:   "reason": "..."
125: }
126: ```
127: 
128: ## Consensus rules (recap from equivalence-check-protocol.md)
129: 
130: | Result | Verdict |
131: |---|---|
132: | 3/3 `equivalent` | PASS high confidence |
133: | 2/3 `equivalent`, 1 `uncertain` | PASS moderate confidence |
134: | 2/3 `equivalent`, 1 `not_equivalent` | **FLAG → Tier 2** |
135: | ≤1 `equivalent` | FAIL → REJECT |
136: | Any `not_equivalent` cites *specific* behavior change | **FLAG → Tier 2** even if 2/3 said equivalent |
137: 
138: Specific behavior diff outranks numeric majority. The gate
139: explicitly does not let majority-rules over a substantive dissent.
140: 
141: ## Token cost
142: 
143: Per refactor round: 3 judge calls × (prompt + 2 outputs) ≈ 3-5K
144: tokens depending on output size.
145: 
146: For a skill with 3 test prompts running 3 rounds: 9 ensembles × 3K
147: ≈ 27K tokens. Reasonable for an automation that's preserving
148: behavior.
149: 
150: ## When ensemble is overkill
151: 
152: If the refactor is purely *structural* and the test prompts are
153: deterministic (file transforms, code generation), Layer 1 alone
154: may be sufficient. You can skip the LLM ensemble if **all three of
155: these hold**:
156: 
157: 1. Layer 1 structural check is exhaustive (covers all output
158:    dimensions)
159: 2. Test prompts are deterministic (same input → same output)
160: 3. The refactor move is from the catalog's "Low" risk tier
161: 
162: In that case, Layer 1 pass = PROCEED. Document in
163: `results.tsv` as `eval_mode: structural_only`.
164: 
165: For any non-deterministic / prose / creative output, run the full
166: ensemble.

FILE skill-refactor/references/package-resource-mode.md
1: # Package-Resource Mode
2: 
3: Use this protocol only when the candidate changes a bundled resource as well
4: as, or instead of, `SKILL.md`. It keeps the entrypoint-mode Q2 threshold out
5: of package accounting: package mode measures the target file and the whole
6: package, so extracted prose is not counted as a saving.
7: 
8: ## Package Q2 — whole-package reduction
9: 
10: Q2 uses **whole-package words** from `account`, not a target-file count.
11: 
12: | Whole-package word reduction | Overall Q2 result |
13: |---|---|
14: | ≥10% | **PROCEED** when the other gates pass |
15: | 5% ≤ reduction <10% | **RESHAPE** only when the other gates pass; the user decides whether to keep the weak win |
16: | <5% or increase | **REJECT** |
17: 
18: Bytes are report-only: retain them for diagnosis, but do not use them for the
19: Q2 threshold. The reducer judges only **layered behavioral evidence**; it does
20: not decide Q2. The overall skill verdict consumes Q2 and the reducer verdict,
21: together with the normal Q1 and Q3 results, before it applies an
22: isolated candidate.
23: 
24: ## Locate the bundled gate
25: 
26: Resolve `scripts/package_gate.py` relative to the loaded `skill-refactor` skill
27: directory into an absolute `<package-gate>` path before running this protocol.
