28: Never derive this path from the target repository or current working directory,
29: and use no Claude-only environment variable or runtime dependency.
30: 
31: ## Safe candidate sequence
32: 
33: 1. Before any candidate edit, export the Git-pinned baseline:
34: 
35:    ```sh
36:    python3 "<package-gate>" export --repo <repo> --workspace <workspace> --skill-path <skill-path> --revision <revision>
37:    ```
38: 
39:    Retain both returned values: the JSON `manifest` path and the external
40:    manifest digest (`manifest_sha256`). Keep that digest outside the baseline
41:    directory as part of the immutable orchestration packet. Never recompute it
42:    from the manifest after export. Treat the returned path as the canonical
43:    manifest path; do not copy it, symlink it, or substitute an alias. Keep the
44:    original Git repository and pinned commit available through final
45:    verification. `verify` first checks the external manifest digest, then
46:    re-resolves the Git tree, so changing or repointing the exported files and
47:    their colocated manifest together cannot manufacture a passing baseline.
48: 2. Create an **isolated candidate** by copying the exported baseline into a
49:    separate workspace. Edit only that copy; do not edit the user's worktree.
50: 3. Verify the frozen baseline before comparing it:
51: 
52:    ```sh
53:    python3 "<package-gate>" verify --manifest <manifest> --manifest-sha256 <manifest-sha256>
54:    ```
55: 
56: 4. Account for the candidate's target and full package:
57: 
58:    ```sh
59:    python3 "<package-gate>" account --manifest <manifest> --manifest-sha256 <manifest-sha256> --candidate-root <candidate-root> --target-file <target-file>
60:    ```
61: 
62:    Accounting consumes the verified snapshot captured during that same
63:    operation; it must not reread mutable baseline files after verification.
64: 
65: 5. Run resource, owning-skill, then package evidence in that order. Submit
66:    the normalized evidence JSON to the reducer; add `--dual-host` when the
67:    package gate requires Claude and Codex replays:
68: 
69:    ```sh
70:    python3 "<package-gate>" reduce [--dual-host] < evidence.json
71:    ```
72: 
73:    The CLI returns JSON and preserves the closed verdict vocabulary:
74:    `PASS`, `FAIL`, or `UNGRADABLE`. A host error is `UNGRADABLE`, never
75:    `PASS`.
76: 6. Combine the reducer result with Q1, whole-package Q2, and Q3. Apply the
77:    isolated candidate only after the overall `PROCEED` verdict. A `RESHAPE`
78:    remains isolated unless the user explicitly accepts the marginal reduction;
79:    record that acceptance as `PROCEED` before applying it. On `FAIL`,
80:    `UNGRADABLE`, or `REJECT`, discard the isolated candidate. Discarding that
81:    copy is the rollback; do not use `git revert` as package-mode evidence.
82: 
83: The `export`, `verify`, `account`, and `reduce` commands are stdlib-only
84: adapters over the tested package-gate APIs. They do not run paid host replays;
85: the caller supplies normalized host evidence to `reduce`.

FILE skill-refactor/references/refactor-moves-catalog.md
1: # Refactor Moves Catalog
2: 
3: Catalog of refactor-hat-safe moves for `skill-refactor`. Each move
4: is a behavior-preserving transformation that should pass the
5: equivalence check. Inspired by Fowler's *Refactoring* catalog
6: applied to SKILL.md prose / structure.
7: 
8: ## Move classification
9: 
10: | Tier | Risk | When safe |
11: |---|---|---|
12: | **Low** | Behavior preservation almost certain | Most moves |
13: | **Medium** | Test rigorously; behavior change possible if move misapplied | Cross-section moves |
14: | **High** | Don't do these without explicit user opt-in | Cosmetic or load-bearing edits |
15: 
16: ## Low-risk moves
17: 
18: ### Dedupe Prose
19: 
20: Same statement appears in multiple places → consolidate to one
21: canonical location, reference from others.
22: 
23: **Example**:
24: - Before: "Read SKILL.md before editing" appears in §1 and §3
25: - After: stated once in §1, §3 says "(see §1)"
26: 
27: **Equivalence risk**: low if the cross-reference is clear.
28: 
29: ### Tighten Phrasing
30: 
31: Verbose constructions → terser equivalents without information
32: loss.
33: 
34: **Examples**:
35: - "in order to" → "to"
36: - "due to the fact that" → "because"
37: - "at this point in time" → "now"
38: - "make sure that you" → (just imperative)
39: 
40: **Equivalence risk**: low. These phrasings are well-known
41: boilerplate.
42: 
43: ### Inline Single-Use Definition
44: 
45: A term defined inline once, used once → just inline the meaning.
46: 
47: **Example**:
48: - Before: "An invariant (a property that must hold across all
49:   rounds) is..."
50: - After: "A property that must hold across all rounds is..."
51: 
52: **Equivalence risk**: low.
53: 
54: ### Compress Repeated Lists into Tables
55: 
56: Three bullet lists with identical structure → one table.
57: 
58: **Example**: see Verdict / Tier section in `SKILL.md` — three
59: bullet patterns compressed to a table reduces ~20% volume.
60: 
61: **Equivalence risk**: low; same content reorganized.
62: 
63: ### Remove Redundant ALWAYS / NEVER Caps
64: 
65: When prose can carry the meaning, the caps add token cost without
66: behavioral signal. Per `skill-creator-advance/SKILL.md` writing
67: style note: "if you find yourself writing ALWAYS or NEVER in all
68: caps, that's a yellow flag — reframe and explain the reasoning".
69: 
70: **Equivalence risk**: low–medium. Test carefully on edge cases.
71: Caps sometimes carry "this is a hard rule" semantic that prose
72: loses.
73: 
74: ## Medium-risk moves
75: 
76: ### Extract to references/
77: 
78: Move a self-contained section (e.g. a worked example, a detailed
79: protocol) from SKILL.md body to a `references/` file. SKILL.md body
80: gets a one-line pointer.
81: 
82: **Constraint**: the reference must be loaded on demand by the
83: skill's flow (mentioned in the right place); otherwise behavior
84: breaks because Claude won't see the content when relevant.
85: 
86: Use package mode for every bundled-resource change, including a new reference
87: required for equivalent extraction. Preserve existing dependencies; verify Q1,
88: Q3 and whole-package Q2 under `package-resource-mode.md`. Moving prose alone earns no reduction.
89: 
90: **Equivalence risk**: medium. Test the use case that the extracted
91: section served — is it still triggered correctly?
92: 
93: **Example**: extracting "Description Optimization" section (~700
94: words) to `references/description-optimization.md`, with a pointer
95: "see references/description-optimization.md when user asks to
96: optimize description triggering".
97: 
98: ### Move Worked Examples to Companion File
99: 
100: When a protocol has 3+ worked examples, extract per the structural convention gates'
101: companion-file convention to `{protocol-name}-examples.md`.
102: 
103: **Constraint**: keep 1-2 examples inline as quick-mode pattern
104: anchor (per domain-team structural convention).
105: 
106: **Equivalence risk**: medium. Quick-mode runs may behave
107: differently if companion file doesn't load. Test both modes.
108: 
109: ### Reorder Sections (No Content Change)
110: 
111: Move a section earlier/later in the file. Useful when current
112: order causes Claude to miss a section because of where it falls in
113: the prompt.
114: 
115: **Equivalence risk**: medium. Order matters for some Claude
116: behaviors; test if the section's effect changes.
117: 
118: ### Leading-Word Substitution
119: 
120: Replace an in-file explanation or derivation with the NAME of a
121: pre-trained concept the model already holds (a Fowler smell name, a
122: Beck pattern, a spec identifier like BCP 47) — the name recruits the
123: model's prior instead of the prose re-teaching it from scratch.
124: 
125: **Guard (mandatory)**: equivalence runs for this move MUST execute
126: on the weakest model tier that will run the target skill in
127: production. Priors vary by tier — a leading word that lands on Opus
128: may not fire on Haiku (Anthropic's own skill best-practices carries
129: the same Haiku-vs-Opus warning; this repo's explicit-contract
130: weak-tier evidence backs it independently). Never sign this move off
131: on a stronger tier than production will actually run.
132: 
133: **Example**:
134: - Before: "Kent Beck's approach where, if a test turns out too big
135:   to make pass in one step, you write a smaller test representing
136:   the broken-off part, get that one green, then return to the
137:   original test — see his book on test-driven development,
138:   published by Addison-Wesley in 2002"
139: - After: "Kent Beck's 'Child Test' pattern (*TDD: By Example*, 2002,
140:   Part III)"
141: - Grounded case: PR #559 compressed a full citation to exactly this
142:   line, with equivalence proven on the weakest tier.
143: 
144: **Equivalence risk**: medium (tier-dependent priors) — proceed only
145: after the weakest-tier run above passes.
146: 
147: Attribution: technique from Matt Pocock's mattpocock/skills
148: `writing-great-skills` (MIT) / aihero.dev.
149: 
150: ## High-risk moves (require explicit user opt-in)
151: 
152: ### Remove Section Considered Redundant
153: 
154: If a section appears redundant with another, remove it.
155: 
156: **Risk**: redundancy might be deliberate emphasis. The first
157: mention plants the concept; the repeat reinforces. Removal can
158: cause Claude to miss the rule.
159: 
160: **Mitigation**: don't auto-remove. Surface to user as "section X
161: seems redundant with section Y; OK to remove?" before doing it.
162: 
163: ### Compress Numbered List to Prose
164: 
165: A 5-item numbered list → a single paragraph.
166: 
167: **Risk**: numbered lists give Claude clear "follow these in order"
168: semantic. Prose loses ordering hints.
169: 
170: **Mitigation**: only do this if the items are not order-sensitive,
171: and verify in test prompts.
172: 
173: ### Replace Specific with Abstract
174: 
175: Specific instructions ("write X to file Y at line Z") → abstract
176: ("save the result").
177: 
178: **Risk**: removes load-bearing specificity. Often a behavior
179: change disguised as refactor.
180: 
181: **Mitigation**: avoid this entirely in refactor scope. If
182: specificity is the problem, that's a redesign — go to
183: skill-creator-advance.
184: 
185: ## Out-of-scope moves (do NOT do in refactor)
186: 
187: These are not refactors. If proposed, route elsewhere:
188: 
189: | Proposed move | Why out of scope | Where it should go |
190: |---|---|---|
191: | Add a new phase | New behavior | `skill-creator-advance` |
192: | Remove a fallback / edge case handler | Behavior change | `skill-creator-advance` (with explicit user decision) |
193: | Change input/output format | Contract change | `skill-creator-advance` |
194: | Reword for "clarity" with judgment call | Taste, not refactor | `skill-tuning` |
195: | Remove "deprecated" instruction | Behavior change | `skill-creator-advance` |
196: | Add example / pattern | Additive | `skill-creator-advance` (or just direct edit if trivial) |
197: | Strengthen / weaken a constraint | Behavior change | `skill-creator-advance` |
198: 
199: ## Move stacking
200: 
201: A single refactor round should ideally apply **one move at a
202: time**. This keeps Q1's diagnosis clean: if equivalence fails, you
203: know which move broke it.
204: 
205: When the user wants several moves in sequence, each move is its
206: own round. Q1/Q2/Q3 run independently for each.
207: 
208: ## Ratchet implications
209: 
210: If a move reduces tokens but its equivalence check is moderate
211: confidence (Layer 1 + 2 `equivalent` + 1 `uncertain`, `PASS_MODERATE`),
212: it can still PROCEED when Q2 and Q3 pass — but
213: this becomes the new baseline for subsequent rounds. Stacking
214: moderate-confidence rounds builds compound risk.
215: 
216: Two `equivalent` plus one `not_equivalent` escalates (`FLAG_TIER_2`);
217: specific behavior dissent takes precedence over vote counts, including ≤1
218: `equivalent`. A dissenting vote is never a moderate-confidence pass.
219: 
220: After 3 consecutive moderate-confidence PROCEEDs, automatically
221: flag for human review of the cumulative diff regardless of
222: individual round verdicts. The aggregate behavior may have drifted
223: even if each round individually passed.
224: 
225: ## Summary table
226: 
227: | Move | Tier | Auto / User-confirm |
228: |---|---|---|
229: | Dedupe prose | Low | Auto |
230: | Tighten phrasing | Low | Auto |
231: | Inline single-use definition | Low | Auto |
232: | Compress lists to tables | Low | Auto |
233: | Remove redundant caps | Low | Auto |
234: | Extract to references/ | Medium | Auto with extra Q1 scrutiny |
235: | Move examples to companion | Medium | Auto with extra Q1 scrutiny |
236: | Reorder sections | Medium | User-confirm before |
237: | Leading-word substitution | Medium | Auto with weakest-tier equivalence run |
238: | Remove redundant section | High | User-confirm explicitly |
239: | Compress list to prose | High | User-confirm explicitly |
240: | Replace specific with abstract | Out-of-scope | Don't |
241: | Add new content | Out-of-scope | Don't |
242: | Remove fallback / handler | Out-of-scope | Don't |
243: | Change format / contract | Out-of-scope | Don't |

FILE skill-refactor/references/test-prompts-schema.md
1: # Test Prompts Schema
2: 
3: > **Shared convention — canonical SoT.** This file is bundled in
4: > both `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-refactor/references/` and
5: > `skill-dev-toolkit:skill-tuning/references/` as functional copies.
6: > This location is the canonical SoT for evolution; same-PR drift
7: > rule mirrors edits to the `skill-tuning` copy.
8: 
9: Schema for the `test-prompts.json` file maintained per skill.
10: 
11: ## Purpose
12: 
13: `test-prompts.json` is the **input set** that drives evaluation
14: work for a skill — equivalence checks (skill-refactor), A/B
15: variant testing (skill-tuning), and creation eval (when
16: skill-creator-advance produces a new skill).
17: 
18: Without test prompts, evaluation tools can't run. The first action
19: of any evaluation skill, if test-prompts.json is missing, is to
20: collaborate with the user to create one.
21: 
22: ## File location
23: 
24: ```
25: <skill-dir>/test-prompts.json
26: ```
27: 
28: Same level as `SKILL.md`. Not bundled inside the skill itself —
29: it's data, not skill content.
30: 
31: ## Schema
32: 
33: ```json
34: {
35:   "skill_name": "<skill-name>",
36:   "schema_version": 1,
37:   "created": "YYYY-MM-DD",
38:   "last_reviewed": "YYYY-MM-DD",
39:   "prompts": [
40:     {
41:       "id": 1,
42:       "category": "happy-path",
43:       "prompt": "What the user would actually type",
44:       "expected_behavior": "Short description of what the skill should do",
45:       "edge_case_dimensions": []
46:     },
47:     {
48:       "id": 2,
49:       "category": "edge-case",
50:       "prompt": "...",
51:       "expected_behavior": "...",
52:       "edge_case_dimensions": ["ambiguous input", "missing context"]
53:     },
54:     {
55:       "id": 3,
56:       "category": "stress",
57:       "prompt": "...",
58:       "expected_behavior": "...",
59:       "edge_case_dimensions": ["incomplete request", "tests intake / clarification flow"]
60:     }
61:   ]
62: }
63: ```
64: 
65: ## Field semantics
66: 
67: ### `skill_name`
68: Matches the `name` in the target skill's frontmatter. Used as
69: sanity check.
70: 
71: ### `schema_version`
72: Currently `1`. Incremented if the schema itself evolves.
73: 
74: ### `created` / `last_reviewed`
75: ISO date. `last_reviewed` should be updated whenever the user
76: reviews and confirms the prompts still represent typical use.
77: Recommendation: re-review every 6 months or after a major skill
78: change.
79: 
80: ### `prompts[].id`
81: Stable integer identifier; used to correlate runs across
82: iterations.
83: 
84: ### `prompts[].category`
85: 
86: One of:
87: 
88: | Category | Purpose |
89: |---|---|
90: | `happy-path` | Most common use case; baseline functionality |
91: | `edge-case` | Tricky input that exercises specific instructions / fallbacks |
92: | `stress` | Deliberately incomplete / ambiguous; tests intake or clarification |
93: 
94: A skill with ≥3 prompts should have at least one of each category
95: when possible.
96: 
97: ### `prompts[].prompt`
98: The literal string a user would say. Should be:
99: 
100: - Realistic (something someone would actually type)
101: - Specific enough that the skill has clear work to do
102: - Not overly long — prompt length isn't being tested
103: 
104: Bad: "Format this data" (too generic)
105: Good: "I have a CSV in ~/Downloads with columns Date, Customer,
106: Amount. Can you sort by date desc and remove rows where Amount is
107: missing?"
108: 
109: ### `prompts[].expected_behavior`
110: 1-2 sentence description of what the skill should produce. Not the
111: expected output verbatim — that would over-specify and prevent
112: legitimate variation.
113: 
114: Example: "Skill should ask the user for the column to sort, then
115: produce a sorted CSV with missing-Amount rows filtered out, and
116: report the row count diff."
117: 
118: ### `prompts[].edge_case_dimensions`
119: For non-happy-path prompts, the specific dimensions being stressed.
120: Helps the eval tool understand what to verify.
121: 
122: Examples:
123: - `"ambiguous input"` — user request can be interpreted multiple ways
124: - `"missing context"` — required information not provided
125: - `"out-of-domain"` — input is on the boundary of the skill's scope
126: - `"contradictory request"` — user's stated goal conflicts with their data
127: - `"large input"` — tests handling of input scale
128: 
129: ## Minimum viable
130: 
131: A `test-prompts.json` with **3 prompts** (1 happy + 1 edge + 1
132: stress) is sufficient for most skills. More is better but
133: diminishing returns; 5-7 is typical for mature skills.
134: 
135: A skill with **fewer than 3 test prompts cannot be evaluated by
136: skill-refactor or skill-tuning** — the evaluation gates self-abort
137: and prompt the user to provide more.
138: 
139: ## Curation responsibility
140: 
141: Test prompts are user-confirmed (or at least user-acknowledged):
142: 
143: 1. The skill's author (or refactor / tuning orchestrator) drafts
144:    prompts based on the skill's documented use cases
145: 2. User reviews — does this look like what real users would type?
146: 3. User confirms or edits
147: 4. Saved as canonical for that skill
148: 
149: LLM-drafted prompts that the user never reviewed are unreliable —
150: they tend to over-fit to what the LLM thinks the skill should
151: handle, rather than what users actually do.
152: 
153: ## Lifecycle
154: 
155: | Event | Action |
156: |---|---|
157: | Skill created | Author drafts initial test-prompts.json (≥3) |
158: | Skill refactored | No prompt changes (refactor preserves behavior) |
159: | Skill tasted (output A/B) | Prompts may be augmented if A/B reveals new use case |
160: | Skill rewritten | Re-review prompts; update `last_reviewed`; possibly augment |
161: | 6 months pass | Recommend re-review |
162: 
163: ## Anti-patterns
164: 
165: | Anti-pattern | Why bad |
166: |---|---|
167: | All prompts are happy-path | Misses edge / stress cases; refactor that breaks edges passes the gate |
168: | Prompts are abstract / one-word | Doesn't trigger skill (Claude shortcuts simple prompts) |
169: | Prompts are LLM-generated and never reviewed | Reflects LLM's projection, not real user behavior |
170: | Prompts have *expected outputs* verbatim | Over-specifies; prevents legitimate output variation |
171: | Prompts duplicate each other (same category, same shape) | Wastes eval budget without adding signal |
172: | Stale prompts (>1 year, never reviewed) | Test the past version of the skill |
173: 
174: ## Cross-skill use
175: 
176: The same `test-prompts.json` is used by:
177: 
178: - `skill-refactor` Q1 baseline + comparison runs
179: - `skill-tuning` for A/B variant runs
180: - `skill-creator-advance` Full Eval Path
181: - `skill-judge` (advisory; doesn't require but can use for context)
182: 
183: Single source per skill = consistent eval signal across tools.

FILE skill-refactor/scripts/equivalence_check.py
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """equivalence_check.py — Layer 1 structural equivalence checker for skill-refactor Q1.
3: 
4: Runs the deterministic structural comparison between baseline and candidate
5: outputs. Layer 2 (LLM-judge ensemble) lives in multi_judge.py and is
6: orchestrated separately by the skill's main agent (it needs subagent
7: spawning, which is environment-specific to Claude Code).
8: 
9: This script handles:
10: - Output type matching
11: - Section structure comparison (`## ` headings)
12: - File path comparison (paths created / referenced)
13: - Tool call comparison (when tool-call traces are provided)
14: - Token count delta within ±20%
15: 
16: Per references/equivalence-check-protocol.md §Layer 1.
17: 
18: Usage:
19:     python equivalence_check.py \\
20:         --baseline-dir <workspace>/baseline/eval-1/ \\
21:         --candidate-dir <workspace>/candidate/eval-1/ \\
22:         [--report <output.json>]
23: 
24: Output:
25:     JSON report to stdout (or --report path):
26:         {
27:             "pass": true | false,
28:             "checks": {
29:                 "output_type_match": true,
30:                 "section_structure": true,
31:                 "file_paths": true,
32:                 "tool_calls": true,
33:                 "token_count_delta": true
34:             },
35:             "details": { ... per-check evidence ... },
36:             "verdict": "PASS_LAYER_1" | "FAIL_LAYER_1"
37:         }
38: """
39: 
40: from __future__ import annotations
41: 
42: import argparse
43: import json
44: import re
45: import sys
46: from pathlib import Path
47: 
48: 
49: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
50: # Output type matching
51: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
52: 
53: EXPECTED_TYPES = {".md", ".json", ".csv", ".txt", ".py", ".js", ".ts", ".html"}
54: 
55: 
56: def output_types_present(directory: Path) -> set[str]:
57:     """Return the set of file extensions in a directory."""
58:     if not directory.exists():
59:         return set()
60:     return {p.suffix.lower() for p in directory.rglob("*") if p.is_file()}
61: 
62: 
63: def check_output_type_match(baseline_dir: Path, candidate_dir: Path) -> tuple[bool, dict]:
64:     baseline_types = output_types_present(baseline_dir)
65:     candidate_types = output_types_present(candidate_dir)
66:     match = baseline_types == candidate_types
67:     return match, {
68:         "baseline_types": sorted(baseline_types),
69:         "candidate_types": sorted(candidate_types),
70:         "missing_in_candidate": sorted(baseline_types - candidate_types),
71:         "extra_in_candidate": sorted(candidate_types - baseline_types),
72:     }
73: 
74: 
75: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
76: # Section structure (markdown only)
77: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
78: 
79: H2_RE = re.compile(r"^##\s+(.+)$", re.MULTILINE)
80: 
81: 
82: def extract_h2_set(md_path: Path) -> set[str]:
83:     if not md_path.exists():
84:         return set()
85:     text = md_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
86:     return {h.strip() for h in H2_RE.findall(text)}
87: 
88: 
89: def check_section_structure(baseline_dir: Path, candidate_dir: Path) -> tuple[bool, dict]:
90:     """Compare the union of all `## ` headings in markdown files between dirs.
91: 
92:     Pass: same set of H2 headings (order may vary).
93:     """
94:     baseline_md = sorted(baseline_dir.rglob("*.md")) if baseline_dir.exists() else []
95:     candidate_md = sorted(candidate_dir.rglob("*.md")) if candidate_dir.exists() else []
96: 
97:     baseline_h2 = set()
98:     for p in baseline_md:
99:         baseline_h2.update(extract_h2_set(p))
100: 
101:     candidate_h2 = set()
102:     for p in candidate_md:
103:         candidate_h2.update(extract_h2_set(p))
104: 
105:     match = baseline_h2 == candidate_h2
106:     return match, {
107:         "baseline_h2_count": len(baseline_h2),
108:         "candidate_h2_count": len(candidate_h2),
109:         "missing_in_candidate": sorted(baseline_h2 - candidate_h2),
110:         "extra_in_candidate": sorted(candidate_h2 - baseline_h2),
111:     }
112: 
113: 
114: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
115: # File path set
116: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
117: 
118: 
119: def file_paths_relative(directory: Path) -> set[str]:
120:     if not directory.exists():
121:         return set()
122:     return {
123:         str(p.relative_to(directory))
124:         for p in directory.rglob("*")
125:         if p.is_file()
126:     }
127: 
128: 
129: def check_file_paths(baseline_dir: Path, candidate_dir: Path) -> tuple[bool, dict]:
130:     baseline_paths = file_paths_relative(baseline_dir)
131:     candidate_paths = file_paths_relative(candidate_dir)
132:     match = baseline_paths == candidate_paths
133:     return match, {
134:         "baseline_count": len(baseline_paths),
135:         "candidate_count": len(candidate_paths),
136:         "missing_in_candidate": sorted(baseline_paths - candidate_paths),
137:         "extra_in_candidate": sorted(candidate_paths - baseline_paths),
138:     }
139: 
140: 
141: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
142: # Tool call comparison (when traces are provided)
143: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
144: 
145: 
146: def load_tool_calls(directory: Path) -> list[str]:
147:     """Read tool_calls.json if present; return list of tool names in invocation order."""
148:     trace_path = directory / "tool_calls.json"
149:     if not trace_path.exists():
150:         return []
151:     try:
152:         data = json.loads(trace_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
153:         return [call.get("tool") for call in data.get("calls", []) if call.get("tool")]
154:     except (json.JSONDecodeError, AttributeError):
155:         return []
156: 
157: 
158: def check_tool_calls(baseline_dir: Path, candidate_dir: Path) -> tuple[bool, dict]:
159:     """Require identical sequences or one insertion/deletion with the same vocabulary."""
160:     baseline_calls = load_tool_calls(baseline_dir)
161:     candidate_calls = load_tool_calls(candidate_dir)
162: 
163:     if not baseline_calls and not candidate_calls:
164:         return True, {"note": "no tool_calls.json provided in either dir; skipping check"}
165: 
166:     baseline_set = set(baseline_calls)
167:     candidate_set = set(candidate_calls)
168:     set_match = baseline_set == candidate_set
169:     count_diff = abs(len(baseline_calls) - len(candidate_calls))
170:     count_within_tolerance = count_diff <= 1
171: 
172:     sequence_match = baseline_calls == candidate_calls
173:     if count_diff == 1:
174:         shorter, longer = sorted((baseline_calls, candidate_calls), key=len)
175:         # The first mismatch must be the only extra call in the longer trace.
176:         mismatch = next(
177:             (index for index, tool in enumerate(shorter) if tool != longer[index]),
178:             len(shorter),
179:         )
180:         sequence_match = shorter[mismatch:] == longer[mismatch + 1:]
181: 
182:     match = set_match and count_within_tolerance and sequence_match
183:     return match, {
184:         "baseline_calls": baseline_calls,
185:         "candidate_calls": candidate_calls,
186:         "set_match": set_match,
187:         "count_diff": count_diff,
188:         "count_within_tolerance": count_within_tolerance,
189:         "sequence_match": sequence_match,
190:     }
191: 
192: 
193: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
194: # Token count delta (±20%)
195: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
196: 
197: 
198: def total_word_count(directory: Path) -> int:
199:     """Sum word counts across all text files in directory."""
200:     if not directory.exists():
201:         return 0
202:     total = 0
203:     for p in directory.rglob("*"):
204:         if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in {".md", ".txt", ".py", ".js", ".ts", ".html", ".json", ".csv"}:
205:             try:
206:                 total += len(p.read_text(encoding="utf-8").split())
207:             except (UnicodeDecodeError, OSError):
208:                 continue
209:     return total
210: 
211: 
212: def check_token_count_delta(baseline_dir: Path, candidate_dir: Path, tolerance: float = 0.20) -> tuple[bool, dict]:
213:     baseline_words = total_word_count(baseline_dir)
214:     candidate_words = total_word_count(candidate_dir)
215: 
216:     if baseline_words == 0:
217:         return False, {"error": "baseline word count is zero; cannot compute delta"}
218: 
219:     delta_pct = abs(candidate_words - baseline_words) / baseline_words
220:     within = delta_pct <= tolerance
221: 
222:     return within, {
223:         "baseline_words": baseline_words,
224:         "candidate_words": candidate_words,
225:         "delta_pct": round(delta_pct * 100, 1),
226:         "tolerance_pct": int(tolerance * 100),
227:     }
228: 
229: 
230: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
231: # Main
232: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
233: 
234: 
235: def run_layer_1(baseline_dir: Path, candidate_dir: Path) -> dict:
236:     type_pass, type_detail = check_output_type_match(baseline_dir, candidate_dir)
237:     structure_pass, structure_detail = check_section_structure(baseline_dir, candidate_dir)
238:     paths_pass, paths_detail = check_file_paths(baseline_dir, candidate_dir)
239:     tools_pass, tools_detail = check_tool_calls(baseline_dir, candidate_dir)
240:     tokens_pass, tokens_detail = check_token_count_delta(baseline_dir, candidate_dir)
241: 
242:     all_pass = all([type_pass, structure_pass, paths_pass, tools_pass, tokens_pass])
243: 
244:     return {
245:         "pass": all_pass,
246:         "verdict": "PASS_LAYER_1" if all_pass else "FAIL_LAYER_1",
247:         "checks": {
248:             "output_type_match": type_pass,
249:             "section_structure": structure_pass,
250:             "file_paths": paths_pass,
251:             "tool_calls": tools_pass,
252:             "token_count_delta": tokens_pass,
253:         },
254:         "details": {
255:             "output_type_match": type_detail,
256:             "section_structure": structure_detail,
257:             "file_paths": paths_detail,
258:             "tool_calls": tools_detail,
259:             "token_count_delta": tokens_detail,
260:         },
261:     }
262: 
263: 
264: def main() -> int:
265:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.split("\n")[0])
266:     parser.add_argument("--baseline-dir", required=True, type=Path)
267:     parser.add_argument("--candidate-dir", required=True, type=Path)
268:     parser.add_argument("--report", type=Path, default=None)
269:     args = parser.parse_args()
270: 
271:     if not args.baseline_dir.exists():
272:         print(f"error: baseline-dir does not exist: {args.baseline_dir}", file=sys.stderr)
273:         return 2
274:     if not args.candidate_dir.exists():
275:         print(f"error: candidate-dir does not exist: {args.candidate_dir}", file=sys.stderr)
276:         return 2
277: 
278:     report = run_layer_1(args.baseline_dir, args.candidate_dir)
279:     output = json.dumps(report, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
280: 
281:     if args.report:
282:         args.report.write_text(output, encoding="utf-8")
283:     else:
284:         print(output)
285: 
286:     return 0 if report["pass"] else 1
287: 
288: 
289: if __name__ == "__main__":
290:     sys.exit(main())

FILE skill-refactor/scripts/golden_compare.py
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """golden_compare.py — compare a candidate output to golden anchors (comparison only).
3: 
4: Q1's 2 equivalent + 1 uncertain, without specific behavior dissent, yields
5: PASS_MODERATE and may proceed under existing Q1/Q2/Q3 rules. This module
6: provides an optional supplemental comparison to a curated golden anchor.
7: If selected, an anchor regression can reject the candidate. A positive
8: comparison cannot clear FLAG_TIER_2 (2 equivalent + 1 not_equivalent, or
9: specific behavior dissent) or replace its required human review.
10: 
11: Per references/golden-anchor-protocol.md §How skill-refactor uses goldens.
12: 
13: This script:
14:     1. Reads golden/ directory for the target skill (frontmatter-tagged
15:        per golden-anchor-protocol.md schema).
16:     2. Filters goldens by `prompt_pattern` matching the test prompt.
17:     3. For each matching golden, computes simple textual similarity
18:        (word-set Jaccard + length ratio) to baseline output and to
19:        candidate output.
20:     4. Emits comparison report.
21: 
22: Note: this is intentionally simple — Jaccard + length is a baseline
23: heuristic, not a semantic similarity metric. Real semantic similarity
24: would require LLM judges (which Layer 2 already does). This script
25: serves the "is candidate at least as close to golden as baseline" check
26: as supplemental evidence, where direction matters more than precision.
27: Legacy TIER_2_PASS / TIER_2_FAIL labels are comparison results only,
28: not adoption authorization; output fields and exit codes remain compatible.
29: 
30: Usage:
31:     # Optional comparison evidence; a positive result never clears FLAG_TIER_2.
32:     python golden_compare.py \\
33:         --golden-dir <skill>/golden/ \\
34:         --baseline-output <workspace>/baseline/eval-1/output.md \\
35:         --candidate-output <workspace>/candidate/eval-1/output.md \\
36:         --prompt-pattern "user request type" \\
37:         [--report <output.json>]
38: 
39: Output:
40:     {
41:       "matching_goldens": ["golden-1", "golden-3", ...],
42:       "baseline_avg_similarity": 0.62,
43:       "candidate_avg_similarity": 0.74,
44:       "candidate_closer_to_golden": true,
45:       "verdict": "TIER_2_PASS" | "TIER_2_FAIL"
46:     }
47: 
48: Exit code: 0 if TIER_2_PASS, 1 if TIER_2_FAIL, 2 on error.
49: """
50: 
51: from __future__ import annotations
52: 
53: import argparse
54: import json
55: import re
56: import sys
57: from pathlib import Path
58: 
59: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
60: # Golden parsing
61: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
62: 
63: FRONTMATTER_RE = re.compile(r"^---\n(.*?)\n---\n(.*)", re.DOTALL)
64: ANCHOR_OUTPUT_RE = re.compile(r"^# Anchor Output\s*\n(.*?)(?=^# |\Z)", re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL)
65: 
66: 
67: def parse_golden(path: Path) -> dict | None:
68:     """Parse a golden file. Returns dict or None if malformed."""
69:     try:
70:         text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
71:     except (UnicodeDecodeError, OSError):
72:         return None
73: 
74:     match = FRONTMATTER_RE.match(text)
75:     if not match:
76:         return None
77: 
78:     frontmatter_text = match.group(1)
79:     body = match.group(2)
80: 
81:     # Naive YAML parse: just lines like `key: value`
82:     fm: dict = {}
83:     for line in frontmatter_text.split("\n"):
84:         if ":" in line and not line.lstrip().startswith("-"):
85:             key, _, value = line.partition(":")
86:             fm[key.strip()] = value.strip()
87: 
88:     # Extract Anchor Output body
89:     output_match = ANCHOR_OUTPUT_RE.search(body)
90:     anchor_output = output_match.group(1).strip() if output_match else ""
91: 
92:     return {
93:         "id": fm.get("id", path.stem),
94:         "prompt_pattern": fm.get("prompt_pattern", ""),
95:         "anchor_output": anchor_output,
96:     }
97: 
98: 
99: def load_goldens(golden_dir: Path) -> list[dict]:
100:     if not golden_dir.exists():
101:         return []
102:     goldens = []
103:     for path in sorted(golden_dir.glob("*.md")):
104:         if path.name == "README.md":
105:             continue
106:         parsed = parse_golden(path)
107:         if parsed:
108:             goldens.append(parsed)
109:     return goldens
110: 
111: 
112: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
113: # Similarity metrics
114: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
115: 
116: WORD_RE = re.compile(r"\b\w+\b")
117: 
118: 
119: def to_word_set(text: str) -> set[str]:
120:     return {w.lower() for w in WORD_RE.findall(text)}
121: 
122: 
123: def jaccard(a: set[str], b: set[str]) -> float:
124:     if not a and not b:
125:         return 1.0
126:     if not a or not b:
127:         return 0.0
128:     return len(a & b) / len(a | b)
129: 
130: 
131: def length_ratio(a: str, b: str) -> float:
132:     """Ratio of shorter/longer, in [0, 1]. 1 = same length."""
133:     la, lb = len(a), len(b)
134:     if la == 0 and lb == 0:
135:         return 1.0
136:     if la == 0 or lb == 0:
137:         return 0.0
138:     return min(la, lb) / max(la, lb)
139: 
140: 
141: def text_similarity(a: str, b: str) -> float:
142:     """Combined Jaccard (word set) + length ratio. Equal weight."""
143:     j = jaccard(to_word_set(a), to_word_set(b))
144:     lr = length_ratio(a, b)
145:     return (j + lr) / 2
146: 
147: 
148: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
149: # Comparison
150: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
151: 
152: 
153: def filter_goldens_by_pattern(goldens: list[dict], prompt_pattern: str) -> list[dict]:
154:     """Filter goldens whose prompt_pattern is a substring of the requested pattern (or vice versa).
155: 
156:     Loose matching since prompt_pattern is human-curated free-form text.
157:     """
158:     if not prompt_pattern:
159:         return goldens
160:     p_lower = prompt_pattern.lower()
161:     return [
162:         g for g in goldens
163:         if p_lower in g["prompt_pattern"].lower() or g["prompt_pattern"].lower() in p_lower
164:     ]
165: 
166: 
167: def compare(golden_dir: Path, baseline_output: str, candidate_output: str, prompt_pattern: str) -> dict:
168:     goldens = load_goldens(golden_dir)
169:     if not goldens:
170:         return {
171:             "matching_goldens": [],
172:             "baseline_avg_similarity": None,
173:             "candidate_avg_similarity": None,
174:             "candidate_closer_to_golden": None,
175:             "verdict": "TIER_2_NO_GOLDENS",
176:             "note": f"no goldens parsed from {golden_dir}; cannot run Tier 2 anchor check",
177:         }
178: 
179:     matching = filter_goldens_by_pattern(goldens, prompt_pattern)
180:     if not matching:
181:         return {
182:             "matching_goldens": [],
183:             "baseline_avg_similarity": None,
184:             "candidate_avg_similarity": None,
185:             "candidate_closer_to_golden": None,
186:             "verdict": "TIER_2_NO_MATCH",
187:             "note": f"no goldens matched prompt_pattern={prompt_pattern!r}; consider adding a matching golden",
188:         }
189: 
190:     baseline_sims = [text_similarity(baseline_output, g["anchor_output"]) for g in matching]
191:     candidate_sims = [text_similarity(candidate_output, g["anchor_output"]) for g in matching]
192: 
193:     baseline_avg = sum(baseline_sims) / len(baseline_sims)
194:     candidate_avg = sum(candidate_sims) / len(candidate_sims)
195: 
196:     closer = candidate_avg >= baseline_avg
197:     verdict = "TIER_2_PASS" if closer else "TIER_2_FAIL"
198: 
199:     return {
200:         "matching_goldens": [g["id"] for g in matching],
201:         "baseline_avg_similarity": round(baseline_avg, 3),
202:         "candidate_avg_similarity": round(candidate_avg, 3),
203:         "candidate_closer_to_golden": closer,
204:         "verdict": verdict,
205:     }
206: 
207: 
208: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
209: # Main
210: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
211: 
212: 
213: def main() -> int:
214:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.split("\n")[0])
215:     parser.add_argument("--golden-dir", required=True, type=Path)
216:     parser.add_argument("--baseline-output", required=True, type=Path)
217:     parser.add_argument("--candidate-output", required=True, type=Path)
218:     parser.add_argument("--prompt-pattern", default="")
219:     parser.add_argument("--report", type=Path, default=None)
220:     args = parser.parse_args()
221: 
222:     if not args.baseline_output.exists():
223:         print(f"error: baseline-output does not exist: {args.baseline_output}", file=sys.stderr)
224:         return 2
225:     if not args.candidate_output.exists():
226:         print(f"error: candidate-output does not exist: {args.candidate_output}", file=sys.stderr)
227:         return 2
228: 
229:     try:
230:         baseline_text = args.baseline_output.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
231:         candidate_text = args.candidate_output.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
232:     except (UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as e:
233:         print(f"error reading outputs: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
234:         return 2
235: 
236:     result = compare(args.golden_dir, baseline_text, candidate_text, args.prompt_pattern)
237:     output = json.dumps(result, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
238: 
239:     if args.report:
240:         args.report.write_text(output, encoding="utf-8")
241:     else:
242:         print(output)
243: 
244:     if result["verdict"] == "TIER_2_PASS":
245:         return 0
246:     if result["verdict"] in {"TIER_2_NO_GOLDENS", "TIER_2_NO_MATCH"}:
247:         return 0  # Skipped, not failed
248:     return 1
249: 
250: 
251: if __name__ == "__main__":
252:     sys.exit(main())

FILE skill-refactor/scripts/multi_judge.py
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """multi_judge.py — Layer 2 LLM-judge ensemble orchestrator for skill-refactor Q1.
3: 
4: This module documents the 3-judge ensemble protocol and provides utilities
5: for aggregating ensemble results. The actual judge calls are spawned by the
6: skill's main agent via Claude Code's Task tool — Python alone cannot reach
7: those subagents — so this script handles consensus aggregation and report
8: formatting, not the judge invocation itself.
9: 
10: Per references/multi-judge-ensemble.md.
11: 
12: Workflow:
13:     1. Main agent spawns 3 Task subagents (one per judge frame:
14:        utility / content / boundary).
15:     2. Each subagent receives the prompt + outputs labeled A / B
16:        (random assignment) and returns a verdict JSON.
17:     3. Main agent collects results into ensemble.json.
18:     4. This script reads ensemble.json and emits the consensus verdict
19:        per references/equivalence-check-protocol.md §Layer 2.
20: 
21: ensemble.json schema:
22:     {
23:       "judges": [
24:         {
25:           "judge_id": 1,
26:           "frame": "utility",
27:           "a_is": "baseline" | "candidate",
28:           "b_is": "candidate" | "baseline",
29:           "verdict": "equivalent" | "not_equivalent" | "uncertain",
30:           "reason": "1-2 sentence reason"
31:         },
32:         ...
33:       ]
34:     }
35: 
36: Usage:
37:     python multi_judge.py --ensemble <path/to/ensemble.json>
38: 
39: Output to stdout:
40:     {
41:       "consensus": "PASS_HIGH" | "PASS_MODERATE" | "FLAG_TIER_2" | "FAIL",
42:       "verdict_counts": {"equivalent": N, "not_equivalent": M, "uncertain": K},
43:       "specific_diff_dissent": true | false,
44:       "reasoning": "..."
45:     }
46: 
47: Exit code: 0 if PASS_HIGH or PASS_MODERATE, 2 if FLAG_TIER_2, 1 if FAIL.
48: """
49: 
50: from __future__ import annotations
51: 
52: import argparse
53: import json
54: import re
55: import sys
56: from pathlib import Path
57: from typing import Literal
58: 
59: VerdictType = Literal["equivalent", "not_equivalent", "uncertain"]
60: ConsensusType = Literal["PASS_HIGH", "PASS_MODERATE", "FLAG_TIER_2", "FAIL"]
61: 
62: 
63: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
64: # Specific-behavior-diff detector
65: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
66: 
67: # Phrases in a judge's reason that indicate specific behavior change rather
68: # than vague taste. If a `not_equivalent` reason matches any of these, the
69: # specific-diff override fires (regardless of numeric majority).
70: SPECIFIC_DIFF_PATTERNS = [
71:     r"\bskip\w*\s+(?:a|the|this|that)?\s*(?:check|step|validation|case)",
72:     r"\bdrop\w*\s+(?:a|the|this|that)?\s*(?:warning|fallback|edge|mention|reference)",
73:     r"\b(?:no|missing|absent|removed)\s+(?:fallback|warning|edge case|reference|invariant)",
74:     r"\b(?:different|changed|altered)\s+(?:format|contract|input|output)",
75:     r"\bdoesn'?t\s+(?:handle|cover|address|process)",
76:     r"\bfails to\b",
77:     r"\bbehavior\s+(?:change|diff|differ)",
78:     r"\b(?:specific|specific\s+to)\s+(?:edge|boundary|fallback)",
79: ]
80: 
81: 
82: def reason_cites_specific_diff(reason: str) -> bool:
83:     if not reason:
84:         return False
85:     lower = reason.lower()
86:     return any(re.search(pattern, lower) for pattern in SPECIFIC_DIFF_PATTERNS)
87: 
88: 
89: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
90: # Consensus rules
91: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
92: 
93: 
94: def aggregate_ensemble(judges: list[dict]) -> dict:
95:     """Apply consensus rules per references/equivalence-check-protocol.md."""
96:     if len(judges) != 3:
97:         return {
98:             "consensus": "FAIL",
99:             "verdict_counts": {},
100:             "specific_diff_dissent": False,
101:             "reasoning": f"expected exactly 3 judges, got {len(judges)}",
102:         }
103: 
104:     counts: dict[str, int] = {"equivalent": 0, "not_equivalent": 0, "uncertain": 0}
105:     for j in judges:
106:         v = j.get("verdict")
107:         if v in counts:
108:             counts[v] += 1
109:         else:
110:             counts.setdefault("invalid", 0)
111:             counts["invalid"] += 1
112: 
113:     if counts.get("invalid"):
114:         return {
115:             "consensus": "FAIL",
116:             "verdict_counts": counts,
117:             "specific_diff_dissent": False,
118:             "reasoning": "one or more judges returned invalid verdict; gate cannot proceed",
119:         }
120: 
121:     # Specific-diff dissent override: if any not_equivalent reason cites
122:     # a specific behavior diff, this outranks numeric majority.
123:     specific_diff_dissent = any(
124:         j.get("verdict") == "not_equivalent" and reason_cites_specific_diff(j.get("reason", ""))
125:         for j in judges
126:     )
127: 
128:     eq, neq, unc = counts["equivalent"], counts["not_equivalent"], counts["uncertain"]
129: 
130:     if specific_diff_dissent and eq < 3:
131:         consensus: ConsensusType = "FLAG_TIER_2"
132:         reasoning = "specific-behavior-diff dissent overrides numeric majority"
133:     elif eq == 3:
134:         consensus = "PASS_HIGH"
135:         reasoning = "3/3 judges agree equivalent"
136:     elif eq == 2 and unc == 1:
137:         consensus = "PASS_MODERATE"
138:         reasoning = "2/3 equivalent + 1 uncertain (no concrete dissent)"
139:     elif eq == 2 and neq == 1:
140:         consensus = "FLAG_TIER_2"
141:         reasoning = "2/3 equivalent but 1 dissents → escalate to human"
142:     elif eq <= 1:
143:         consensus = "FAIL"
144:         reasoning = "≤1 judge agrees equivalent; refactor not behavior-preserving"
145:     else:
146:         consensus = "FAIL"
147:         reasoning = f"unexpected vote distribution: {counts}"
148: 
149:     return {
150:         "consensus": consensus,
151:         "verdict_counts": counts,
152:         "specific_diff_dissent": specific_diff_dissent,
153:         "reasoning": reasoning,
154:     }
155: 
156: 
157: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
158: # Main
159: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
160: 
161: 
162: def main() -> int:
163:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.split("\n")[0])
164:     parser.add_argument("--ensemble", required=True, type=Path,
165:                         help="Path to ensemble.json with 3 judge results")
166:     args = parser.parse_args()
167: 
168:     if not args.ensemble.exists():
169:         print(f"error: ensemble file does not exist: {args.ensemble}", file=sys.stderr)
170:         return 2
171: 
172:     try:
173:         data = json.loads(args.ensemble.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
174:     except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
175:         print(f"error: invalid JSON in ensemble file: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
176:         return 2
177: 
178:     judges = data.get("judges", [])
179:     result = aggregate_ensemble(judges)
180:     print(json.dumps(result, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
181: 
182:     consensus = result["consensus"]
183:     if consensus in {"PASS_HIGH", "PASS_MODERATE"}:
184:         return 0
185:     if consensus == "FLAG_TIER_2":
186:         return 2
187:     return 1
188: 
189: 
190: if __name__ == "__main__":
191:     sys.exit(main())

FILE skill-refactor/scripts/package_gate.py
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """Freeze and verify a skill-package baseline exported from Git."""
3: 
4: from __future__ import annotations
5: 
6: import argparse
7: import hashlib
8: import io
9: import json
10: import os
11: from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath
12: import subprocess
13: import sys
14: import tarfile
15: 
16: 
17: PACKAGE_VERDICTS = frozenset({"PASS", "FAIL", "UNGRADABLE"})
18: _CHEAP_LAYERS = ("resource", "owning-skill", "package")
19: _DUAL_HOSTS = ("claude", "codex")
20: 
21: 
22: def _git(repo: Path, *args: str) -> str:
23:     return subprocess.run(
24:         ("git", "-C", str(repo), *args),
25:         check=True,
26:         capture_output=True,
27:         text=True,
28:     ).stdout.strip()
29: 
30: 
31: def _resolved_commit(repo: Path, revision: str) -> str:
32:     result = subprocess.run(
33:         ("git", "-C", str(repo), "rev-parse", "--verify", "--end-of-options", f"{revision}^{{commit}}"),
34:         check=False,
35:         capture_output=True,
36:         text=True,
37:     )
38:     commit = result.stdout.strip()
39:     if result.returncode or len(commit) != 40:
40:         raise ValueError(f"invalid Git revision: {revision}")
41:     return commit
42: 
43: 
44: def _skill_path(skill_path: str) -> PurePosixPath:
45:     path = PurePosixPath(skill_path)
46:     if path.is_absolute() or not skill_path or ".." in path.parts:
47:         raise ValueError(f"invalid skill path: {skill_path}")
48:     return path
49: 
50: 
51: def _file_snapshot(
52:     root: Path,
53: ) -> tuple[dict[str, bytes], dict[str, dict[str, object]]]:
54:     if not root.is_dir() or root.is_symlink():
55:         raise ValueError(f"invalid package root: {root}")
56: 
57:     contents: dict[str, bytes] = {}
58:     files: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = {}
59:     for path in sorted(root.rglob("*")):
60:         if path.is_symlink():
61:             raise ValueError(f"package contains symlink: {path.relative_to(root)}")
62:         if path.is_file():
63:             relative = str(path.relative_to(root))
64:             with path.open("rb") as stream:
65:                 before = os.fstat(stream.fileno())
66:                 content = stream.read()
67:                 after = os.fstat(stream.fileno())
68:             if (before.st_size, before.st_mtime_ns, before.st_mode) != (
69:                 after.st_size,
70:                 after.st_mtime_ns,
71:                 after.st_mode,
72:             ):
73:                 raise ValueError(f"package file changed while reading: {relative}")
74:             contents[relative] = content
75:             files[relative] = {
76:                 "sha256": hashlib.sha256(content).hexdigest(),
77:                 "executable": bool(after.st_mode & 0o111),
78:             }
79:     return contents, files
80: 
81: 
82: def _file_hashes(root: Path) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
83:     return _file_snapshot(root)[1]
84: 
85: 
86: def _file_contents(root: Path) -> dict[str, bytes]:
87:     return _file_snapshot(root)[0]
88: 
89: 
90: def _counts(contents: dict[str, bytes]) -> dict[str, int]:
91:     return {
92:         "words": sum(len(content.decode("utf-8", errors="replace").split()) for content in contents.values()),
93:         "bytes": sum(len(content) for content in contents.values()),
94:     }
95: 
96: 
97: def _delta(baseline: int, candidate: int) -> dict[str, int]:
98:     return {"baseline": baseline, "candidate": candidate, "delta": candidate - baseline}
99: 
100: 
101: def _extract_skill(archive: bytes, destination: Path) -> None:
102:     with tarfile.open(fileobj=io.BytesIO(archive), mode="r:") as tar:
103:         members = tar.getmembers()
104:         for member in members:
105:             path = PurePosixPath(member.name)
106:             if path.is_absolute() or ".." in path.parts or member.issym() or member.islnk():
107:                 raise ValueError(f"unsafe baseline archive member: {member.name}")
108:             if not (member.isdir() or member.isfile()):
109:                 raise ValueError(f"unsupported baseline archive member: {member.name}")
110:         tar.extractall(destination, members=members, filter="data")
111: 
112: 
113: def _archive_file_hashes(archive: bytes) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
114:     """Fingerprint regular files directly from a trusted Git archive."""
115:     files: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = {}
116:     with tarfile.open(fileobj=io.BytesIO(archive), mode="r:") as tar:
117:         for member in tar.getmembers():
118:             path = PurePosixPath(member.name)
119:             if path.is_absolute() or ".." in path.parts or member.issym() or member.islnk():
120:                 raise ValueError(f"unsafe baseline archive member: {member.name}")
121:             if member.isdir():
122:                 continue
123:             if not member.isfile():
124:                 raise ValueError(f"unsupported baseline archive member: {member.name}")
125:             stream = tar.extractfile(member)
126:             if stream is None:
127:                 raise ValueError(f"unreadable baseline archive member: {member.name}")
128:             files[path.as_posix()] = {
129:                 "sha256": hashlib.sha256(stream.read()).hexdigest(),
130:                 "executable": bool(member.mode & 0o111),
131:             }
132:     return files
133: 
134: 
135: def export_baseline(repo: Path, workspace: Path, skill_path: str, revision: str) -> Path:
136:     """Export one skill from *revision* and return its immutable manifest path."""
137:     relative_path = _skill_path(skill_path)
138:     commit = _resolved_commit(repo, revision)
139:     tree = _git(repo, "rev-parse", f"{commit}:{relative_path.as_posix()}")
140:     _git(repo, "cat-file", "-e", f"{commit}:{relative_path.as_posix()}/SKILL.md")
141: 
142:     baseline = workspace / "baseline"
143:     manifest_path = baseline / "manifest.json"
144:     skill_root = baseline / "skill"
145:     if manifest_path.exists() or skill_root.exists():
146:         raise ValueError("baseline already exists; refusing to replace it")
147: 
148:     archive = subprocess.run(
149:         ("git", "-C", str(repo), "archive", "--format=tar", f"{commit}:{relative_path.as_posix()}"),
150:         check=True,
151:         capture_output=True,
152:     ).stdout
153:     baseline.mkdir(parents=True)
154:     try:
155:         _extract_skill(archive, skill_root)
156:         manifest = {
157:             "repository": str(repo.resolve()),
158:             "skill_path": relative_path.as_posix(),
159:             "resolved_commit": commit,
160:             "skill_tree": tree,
161:             "files": _file_hashes(skill_root),
162:         }
163:         manifest_path.write_text(
164:             json.dumps(manifest, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n", encoding="utf-8"
165:         )
166:     except Exception:
167:         if skill_root.exists():
168:             for path in sorted(skill_root.rglob("*"), reverse=True):
169:                 if path.is_file() or path.is_symlink():
170:                     path.unlink()
171:                 elif path.is_dir():
172:                     path.rmdir()
173:             skill_root.rmdir()
174:         raise
175:     return manifest_path.resolve()
176: 
177: 
178: def _verified_baseline_snapshot(
179:     manifest_path: Path, manifest_sha256: str
180: ) -> tuple[
181:     dict[str, bytes] | None,
182:     dict[str, dict[str, object]] | None,
183:     dict[str, str],
184: ]:
185:     try:
186:         if not manifest_path.is_absolute() or manifest_path.resolve() != manifest_path:
187:             return None, None, {
188:                 "verdict": "REFUSED",
189:                 "reason": "manifest path is not canonical",
190:             }
191:         manifest_bytes = manifest_path.read_bytes()
192:         if hashlib.sha256(manifest_bytes).hexdigest() != manifest_sha256:
193:             return None, None, {
194:                 "verdict": "REFUSED",
195:                 "reason": "manifest digest mismatch",
196:             }
197:         manifest = json.loads(manifest_bytes.decode("utf-8"))
198:         repository = manifest["repository"]
199:         skill_path = manifest["skill_path"]
200:         commit = manifest["resolved_commit"]
201:         tree = manifest["skill_tree"]
202:         expected = manifest["files"]
203:         if (
204:             not isinstance(repository, str)
205:             or not isinstance(skill_path, str)
206:             or not isinstance(commit, str)
207:             or not isinstance(tree, str)
208:             or not isinstance(expected, dict)
209:             or not all(
210:             isinstance(path, str)
211:             and isinstance(fingerprint, dict)
212:             and set(fingerprint) == {"sha256", "executable"}
213:             and isinstance(fingerprint["sha256"], str)
214:             and isinstance(fingerprint["executable"], bool)
215:             for path, fingerprint in expected.items()
216:             )
217:         ):
218:             raise ValueError("invalid file fingerprint manifest")
219:         repo = Path(repository)
220:         relative_path = _skill_path(skill_path)
221:         if _resolved_commit(repo, commit) != commit:
222:             raise ValueError("resolved commit no longer matches Git")
223:         if _git(repo, "rev-parse", f"{commit}:{relative_path.as_posix()}") != tree:
224:             raise ValueError("skill tree no longer matches Git")
225:         archive = subprocess.run(
226:             ("git", "-C", str(repo), "archive", "--format=tar", f"{commit}:{relative_path.as_posix()}"),
227:             check=True,
228:             capture_output=True,
229:         ).stdout
230:         git_expected = _archive_file_hashes(archive)
231:         actual_contents, actual = _file_snapshot(manifest_path.parent / "skill")
232:     except (OSError, ValueError, json.JSONDecodeError, KeyError, subprocess.CalledProcessError) as error:
233:         return None, None, {
234:             "verdict": "REFUSED",
235:             "reason": f"baseline verification failed: {error}",
236:         }
237: 
238:     if git_expected != expected:
239:         return None, None, {
240:             "verdict": "REFUSED",
241:             "reason": "Git baseline drift detected",
242:         }
243:     if actual != expected:
244:         return None, None, {
245:             "verdict": "REFUSED",
246:             "reason": "baseline drift detected",
247:         }
248:     return actual_contents, actual, {
249:         "verdict": "PASS",
250:         "reason": "baseline matches manifest",
251:     }
252: 
253: 
254: def verify_baseline(manifest_path: Path, manifest_sha256: str) -> dict[str, str]:
255:     """Return PASS when exported bytes match the manifest, otherwise REFUSED."""
256:     return _verified_baseline_snapshot(manifest_path, manifest_sha256)[2]
257: 
258: 
259: def account_package(
260:     manifest_path: Path,
261:     manifest_sha256: str,
262:     candidate_root: Path,
263:     target_file: str,
264: ) -> dict[str, object]:
265:     """Report target and whole-package word/byte deltas from a verified baseline."""
266:     baseline_files, baseline_fingerprints, verification = _verified_baseline_snapshot(
267:         manifest_path, manifest_sha256
268:     )
269:     if verification["verdict"] != "PASS":
270:         return verification
271: 
272:     try:
273:         target = _skill_path(target_file).as_posix()
274:         if baseline_files is None or baseline_fingerprints is None:
275:             raise ValueError("verified baseline snapshot is unavailable")
276:         candidate_files, candidate_fingerprints = _file_snapshot(candidate_root)
277:         baseline_target = baseline_files[target]
278:         candidate_target = candidate_files[target]
279:     except (KeyError, OSError, ValueError) as error:
280:         return {"verdict": "REFUSED", "reason": f"package accounting failed: {error}"}
281: 
282:     mode_drift = sorted(
283:         path
284:         for path in baseline_fingerprints.keys() & candidate_fingerprints.keys()
285:         if baseline_fingerprints[path]["executable"]
286:         != candidate_fingerprints[path]["executable"]
287:     )
288:     if mode_drift:
289:         return {
290:             "verdict": "REFUSED",
291:             "reason": f"candidate executable mode drift detected: {', '.join(mode_drift)}",
292:         }
293: 
294:     baseline_counts = _counts(baseline_files)
295:     candidate_counts = _counts(candidate_files)
296:     baseline_target_counts = _counts({target: baseline_target})
297:     candidate_target_counts = _counts({target: candidate_target})
298:     return {
299:         "verdict": "PASS",
300:         "target": {
301:             "words": _delta(baseline_target_counts["words"], candidate_target_counts["words"]),
302:             "bytes": _delta(baseline_target_counts["bytes"], candidate_target_counts["bytes"]),
303:         },
304:         "package": {
305:             "words": _delta(baseline_counts["words"], candidate_counts["words"]),
306:             "bytes": _delta(baseline_counts["bytes"], candidate_counts["bytes"]),
307:         },
308:     }
309: 
310: 
311: def _ungradeable(reason: str, accounting: object = None) -> dict[str, object]:
312:     """Return only package-mode verdicts, including malformed evidence."""
313:     result: dict[str, object] = {"verdict": "UNGRADABLE", "reason": reason}
314:     if accounting is not None:
315:         result["accounting"] = accounting
316:     return result
317: 
318: 
319: def _layer_verdict(records: object) -> str:
320:     if not isinstance(records, list) or not records:
321:         raise ValueError("layer needs at least one verdict record")
322:     verdicts = []
323:     for record in records:
324:         if not isinstance(record, dict) or set(record) != {"verdict"}:
325:             raise ValueError("layer record must contain only verdict")
326:         verdict = record["verdict"]
327:         if verdict not in PACKAGE_VERDICTS:
328:             raise ValueError("layer record has an invalid verdict")
329:         verdicts.append(verdict)
330:     if "UNGRADABLE" in verdicts:
331:         return "UNGRADABLE"
332:     return "FAIL" if "FAIL" in verdicts else "PASS"
333: 
334: 
335: def _normalize_host_evidence(records: object) -> list[dict[str, object]]:
336:     if not isinstance(records, list):
337:         raise ValueError("host_evidence must be a list")
338:     normalized = []
339:     for record in records:
340:         if not isinstance(record, dict):
341:             raise ValueError("host evidence must be an object")
342:         expected = {"host", "replicate"}
343:         if not expected <= set(record) or not set(record) <= expected | {"verdict", "error"}:
344:             raise ValueError("host evidence has an invalid schema")
345:         host, replicate = record["host"], record["replicate"]
346:         if host not in _DUAL_HOSTS or not isinstance(replicate, int) or isinstance(replicate, bool) or replicate < 0:
347:             raise ValueError("host evidence has an invalid host or replicate")
348:         if "error" in record:
349:             if not isinstance(record["error"], str) or not record["error"]:
350:                 raise ValueError("host error must be a non-empty string")
351:             normalized.append({"host": host, "replicate": replicate, "verdict": "UNGRADABLE", "error": record["error"]})
352:             continue
353:         if set(record) != expected | {"verdict"} or record["verdict"] not in PACKAGE_VERDICTS:
354:             raise ValueError("host evidence must contain one closed verdict or an error")
355:         normalized.append({"host": host, "replicate": replicate, "verdict": record["verdict"]})
356:     return normalized
357: 
358: 
359: def reduce_package_evidence(evidence: object, *, dual_host: bool = False) -> dict[str, object]:
360:     """Reduce normalized package evidence without invoking a host.
361: 
362:     The input boundary is intentionally closed: adapters must normalize their
363:     observations into the documented layer and host records before this pure
364:     reducer sees them.  This keeps host invocation and paid replay outside the
365:     package gate while making malformed or failed host evidence non-passing.
366:     """
367:     if not isinstance(evidence, dict):
368:         return _ungradeable("evidence must be an object")
369:     expected = {"accounting", *_CHEAP_LAYERS, "host_evidence"}
370:     accounting = evidence.get("accounting")
371:     if set(evidence) != expected:
372:         return _ungradeable("evidence has an invalid schema", accounting)
373:     if not isinstance(accounting, dict) or accounting.get("verdict") != "PASS":
374:         return _ungradeable("accounting must be a passing report", accounting)
375: 
376:     try:
377:         layers = [
378:             {"layer": layer, "verdict": _layer_verdict(evidence[layer])}
379:             for layer in _CHEAP_LAYERS
380:         ]
381:     except ValueError as error:
382:         return _ungradeable(str(error), accounting)
383:     layers[-1]["accounting"] = accounting
384: 
385:     cheap_verdicts = [layer["verdict"] for layer in layers]
386:     if "UNGRADABLE" in cheap_verdicts:
387:         return {"verdict": "UNGRADABLE", "layers": layers, "host_evidence": []}
388:     if "FAIL" in cheap_verdicts:
389:         return {"verdict": "FAIL", "layers": layers, "host_evidence": []}
390: 
391:     try:
392:         hosts = _normalize_host_evidence(evidence["host_evidence"])
393:     except ValueError as error:
394:         return _ungradeable(str(error), accounting)
395:     if any(record["verdict"] == "UNGRADABLE" for record in hosts):
396:         verdict = "UNGRADABLE"
397:     elif dual_host and any(
398:         len({record["replicate"] for record in hosts if record["host"] == host}) < 2
399:         for host in _DUAL_HOSTS
400:     ):
401:         verdict = "UNGRADABLE"
402:     elif any(record["verdict"] == "FAIL" for record in hosts):
403:         verdict = "FAIL"
404:     else:
405:         verdict = "PASS"
406:     return {"verdict": verdict, "layers": layers, "host_evidence": hosts}
407: 
408: 
409: def _cli_result(result: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, object]:
410:     """Keep the CLI boundary within the package-mode verdict vocabulary."""
411:     if result.get("verdict") == "REFUSED":
412:         result = {**result, "verdict": "UNGRADABLE"}
413:     return result
414: 
415: 
416: def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
417:     """Expose package-gate APIs as one JSON stdin/stdout CLI."""
418:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
419:     commands = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
420: 
421:     export = commands.add_parser("export")
422:     export.add_argument("--repo", required=True)
423:     export.add_argument("--workspace", required=True)
424:     export.add_argument("--skill-path", required=True)
425:     export.add_argument("--revision", required=True)
426: 
427:     verify = commands.add_parser("verify")
428:     verify.add_argument("--manifest", required=True)
429:     verify.add_argument("--manifest-sha256", required=True)
430: 
431:     account = commands.add_parser("account")
432:     account.add_argument("--manifest", required=True)
433:     account.add_argument("--manifest-sha256", required=True)
434:     account.add_argument("--candidate-root", required=True)
435:     account.add_argument("--target-file", required=True)
436: 
437:     reduce = commands.add_parser("reduce")
438:     reduce.add_argument("--dual-host", action="store_true")
439: 
440:     args = parser.parse_args(argv)
441:     try:
442:         if args.command == "export":
443:             manifest_path = export_baseline(
444:                 Path(args.repo), Path(args.workspace), args.skill_path, args.revision
445:             )
446:             result: dict[str, object] = {
447:                 "verdict": "PASS",
448:                 "manifest": str(manifest_path),
449:                 "manifest_sha256": hashlib.sha256(manifest_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
450:             }
451:         elif args.command == "verify":
452:             result = verify_baseline(Path(args.manifest), args.manifest_sha256)
453:         elif args.command == "account":
454:             result = account_package(
455:                 Path(args.manifest),
456:                 args.manifest_sha256,
457:                 Path(args.candidate_root),
458:                 args.target_file,
459:             )
460:         else:
461:             result = reduce_package_evidence(json.load(sys.stdin), dual_host=args.dual_host)
462:     except (OSError, ValueError, subprocess.CalledProcessError, json.JSONDecodeError) as error:
463:         result = {"verdict": "UNGRADABLE", "reason": str(error)}
464: 
465:     print(json.dumps(_cli_result(result), sort_keys=True))
466:     return 0
467: 
468: 
469: if __name__ == "__main__":
470:     raise SystemExit(main())

24: def test_exported_baseline_is_revision_bound_and_drift_refuses(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
25:     """Ground the Git surfaces used by export_baseline.
26: 
27:     This session verified `git rev-parse -h`, `git cat-file -h`, and
28:     `git archive -h`; their durable references are
29:     https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rev-parse,
30:     https://git-scm.com/docs/git-cat-file, and
31:     https://git-scm.com/docs/git-archive.
32:     """
33:     from package_gate import export_baseline, verify_baseline
34: 
35:     repo = tmp_path / "repo"
36:     skill_dir = repo / "skills" / "demo"
37:     skill_dir.mkdir(parents=True)
38:     (skill_dir / "SKILL.md").write_bytes(b"---\nname: demo\n---\n\nOriginal\n")
39:     (skill_dir / "resource.txt").write_bytes(b"immutable resource\n")
40:     _git(repo, "init", "-q")
41:     _git(repo, "add", ".")
42:     _git(repo, "-c", "user.name=Test", "-c", "user.email=test@example.com", "commit", "-qm", "baseline")
43:     commit = _git(repo, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
44: 
45:     manifest_path = export_baseline(repo, tmp_path / "workspace", "skills/demo", commit)
46:     manifest_sha256 = _manifest_sha256(manifest_path)
47:     manifest = json.loads(manifest_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
48: 
49:     assert manifest["resolved_commit"] == commit
50:     assert manifest["skill_tree"] == _git(repo, "rev-parse", f"{commit}:skills/demo")
51:     assert manifest["files"] == {
52:         "SKILL.md": {
53:             "sha256": hashlib.sha256(b"---\nname: demo\n---\n\nOriginal\n").hexdigest(),
54:             "executable": False,
55:         },
56:         "resource.txt": {
57:             "sha256": hashlib.sha256(b"immutable resource\n").hexdigest(),
58:             "executable": False,
59:         },
60:     }
61: 
62:     exported_file = manifest_path.parent / "skill" / "SKILL.md"
63:     exported_file.write_bytes(b"drifted bytes\n")
64: 
65:     result = verify_baseline(manifest_path, manifest_sha256)
66: 
67:     assert result["verdict"] == "REFUSED"
68:     assert "drift" in result["reason"]
69:     assert exported_file.read_bytes() == b"drifted bytes\n"

72: def test_baseline_fingerprint_includes_executable_mode(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
73:     from package_gate import export_baseline, verify_baseline
74: 
75:     repo = tmp_path / "repo"
76:     skill_dir = repo / "skills" / "demo"
77:     skill_dir.mkdir(parents=True)
78:     (skill_dir / "SKILL.md").write_text("skill\n", encoding="utf-8")
79:     executable = skill_dir / "run.sh"
80:     executable.write_bytes(b"#!/bin/sh\n")
81:     executable.chmod(0o755)

83:     _git(repo, "add", ".")
84:     _git(repo, "-c", "user.name=Test", "-c", "user.email=test@example.com", "commit", "-qm", "baseline")

86:     manifest_path = export_baseline(repo, tmp_path / "workspace", "skills/demo", "HEAD")

100: def test_baseline_verification_reanchors_a_mutated_manifest_to_git(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
101:     from package_gate import export_baseline, verify_baseline
102: 
103:     repo = tmp_path / "repo"
104:     skill_dir = repo / "skills" / "demo"
105:     skill_dir.mkdir(parents=True)
106:     original = b"---\nname: demo\n---\n\nOriginal\n"
107:     (skill_dir / "SKILL.md").write_bytes(original)
108:     _git(repo, "init", "-q")
109:     _git(repo, "add", ".")
110:     _git(repo, "-c", "user.name=Test", "-c", "user.email=test@example.com", "commit", "-qm", "baseline")
111:     manifest_path = export_baseline(repo, tmp_path / "workspace", "skills/demo", "HEAD")
112:     manifest_sha256 = _manifest_sha256(manifest_path)
113: 
114:     drifted = b"---\nname: demo\n---\n\nDrifted\n"

121:

126: def test_baseline_verification_refuses_full_provenance_repoint(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
127:     from package_gate import export_baseline, verify_baseline
128: 
129:     original_repo = tmp_path / "original-repo"
130:     original_skill = original_repo / "skills" / "demo"
131:     original_skill.mkdir(parents=True)
132:     (original_skill / "SKILL.md").write_text("original\n", encoding="utf-8")
133:     _git(original_repo, "init", "-q")
134:     _git(original_repo, "add", ".")
135:     _git(original_repo, "-c", "user.name=Test", "-c", "user.email=test@example.com", "commit", "-qm", "original")
136:     manifest_path = export_baseline(
137:         original_repo, tmp_path / "workspace", "skills/demo", "HEAD"
138:     )
139:     manifest_sha256 = _manifest_sha256(manifest_path)
140: 
141:     replacement_repo = tmp_path / "replacement-repo"
142:     replacement_skill = replacement_repo / "skills" / "demo"
143:     replacement_skill.mkdir(parents=True)
144:     replacement_bytes = b"replacement\n"
145:     (replacement_skill / "SKILL.md").write_bytes(replacement_bytes)
146:     _git(replacement_repo, "init", "-q")
147:     _git(replacement_repo, "add", ".")
148:     _git(replacement_repo, "-c", "user.name=Test", "-c", "user.email=test@example.com", "commit", "-qm", "replacement")
149:     replacement_commit = _git(replacement_repo, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
150: 
151:     (manifest_path.parent / "skill" / "SKILL.md").write_bytes(replacement_bytes)
152:     manifest = json.loads(manifest_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
153:     manifest.update(
154:         repository=str(replacement_repo.resolve()),
155:         resolved_commit=replacement_commit,
156:         skill_tree=_git(
157:             replacement_repo, "rev-parse", f"{replacement_commit}:skills/demo"
158:         ),
159:     )
160:     manifest["files"]["SKILL.md"]["sha256"] = hashlib.sha256(
161:         replacement_bytes
162:     ).hexdigest()
163:     manifest_path.write_text(json.dumps(manifest), encoding="utf-8")
164: 
165:     assert verify_baseline(manifest_path, manifest_sha256) == {
166:         "verdict": "REFUSED",
167:         "reason": "manifest digest mismatch",
168:     }

193: def test_accounting_uses_the_verified_baseline_snapshot(
194:     tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch
195: ) -> None:
196:     import package_gate
197: 
198:     repo = tmp_path / "repo"
199:     skill_dir = repo / "skills" / "demo"
200:     skill_dir.mkdir(parents=True)
201:     (skill_dir / "SKILL.md").write_text("one two\n", encoding="utf-8")
202:     _git(repo, "init", "-q")
203:     _git(repo, "add", ".")
204:     _git(repo, "-c", "user.name=Test", "-c", "user.email=test@example.com", "commit", "-qm", "baseline")
205:     manifest_path = package_gate.export_baseline(
206:         repo, tmp_path / "workspace", "skills/demo", "HEAD"
207:     )
208:     manifest_sha256 = _manifest_sha256(manifest_path)
209:     candidate = tmp_path / "candidate"
210:     candidate.mkdir()
211:     (candidate / "SKILL.md").write_text("one\n", encoding="utf-8")
212: 
213:     original_verify = package_gate.verify_baseline
214: 
215:     def mutate_after_verification(path: Path, digest: str):
216:         verdict = original_verify(path, digest)
217:         (path.parent / "skill" / "SKILL.md").write_text(
218:             "attacker controlled baseline words\n", encoding="utf-8"
219:         )
220:         return verdict
221: 
222:     monkeypatch.setattr(package_gate, "verify_baseline", mutate_after_verification)
223: 
224:     result = package_gate.account_package(
225:         manifest_path, manifest_sha256, candidate, "SKILL.md"
226:     )
227: 
228:     assert result["verdict"] == "PASS"
229:     assert result["package"]["words"]["baseline"] == 2

301: def test_dual_host_error_is_ungradeable_and_blocks_package_pass() -> None:
302:     """A host failure is missing behavioral evidence, never a passing replay."""
303:     from package_gate import reduce_package_evidence
304: 
305:     accounting = {
306:         "verdict": "PASS",
307:         "target": {"words": {"baseline": 10, "candidate": 8, "delta": -2}},
308:         "package": {"words": {"baseline": 20, "candidate": 18, "delta": -2}},
309:     }
310:     result = reduce_package_evidence(
311:         {
312:             "resource": [{"verdict": "PASS"}],
313:             "owning-skill": [{"verdict": "PASS"}],
314:             "package": [{"verdict": "PASS"}],
315:             "host_evidence": [
316:                 {"host": "claude", "replicate": 0, "verdict": "PASS"},
317:                 {"host": "claude", "replicate": 1, "verdict": "PASS"},
318:                 {"host": "codex", "replicate": 0, "verdict": "PASS"},
319:                 {"host": "codex", "replicate": 1, "error": "host exited with status 1"},
320:             ],
321:             "accounting": accounting,
322:         },
323:         dual_host=True,
324:     )
325: 
326:     assert result["verdict"] == "UNGRADABLE"
327:     assert [layer["layer"] for layer in result["layers"]] == [
328:         "resource",
329:         "owning-skill",
330:         "package",
331:     ]
332:     assert result["layers"][-1]["accounting"] == accounting
333:     assert result["host_evidence"][-1]["verdict"] == "UNGRADABLE"
334:

336: def test_cli_drives_export_verify_and_account(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
337:     repo = tmp_path / "repo"
338:     skill_dir = repo / "skills" / "demo"
339:     skill_dir.mkdir(parents=True)
340:     (skill_dir / "SKILL.md").write_text("original words\n", encoding="utf-8")
341:     _git(repo, "init", "-q")
342:     _git(repo, "add", ".")
343:     _git(repo, "-c", "user.name=Test", "-c", "user.email=test@example.com", "commit", "-qm", "baseline")
344: 
345:     script = SCRIPTS_DIR / "package_gate.py"
346:     export = subprocess.run(
347:         [
348:             sys.executable, str(script), "export", "--repo", str(repo),
349:             "--workspace", str(tmp_path / "workspace"), "--skill-path", "skills/demo",
350:             "--revision", "HEAD",
351:         ],
352:         check=True, capture_output=True, text=True,
353:     )
354:     export_result = json.loads(export.stdout)
355:     manifest = Path(export_result["manifest"])
356:     manifest_sha256 = export_result["manifest_sha256"]
357: 
358:     verify = subprocess.run(
359:         [
360:             sys.executable, str(script), "verify", "--manifest", str(manifest),
361:             "--manifest-sha256", manifest_sha256,
362:         ],
363:         check=True, capture_output=True, text=True,
364:     )
365:     assert json.loads(verify.stdout)["verdict"] == "PASS"
366: 
367:     candidate = tmp_path / "candidate"
368:     candidate.mkdir()
369:     (candidate / "SKILL.md").write_text("smaller\n", encoding="utf-8")
370:     account = subprocess.run(
371:         [
372:             sys.executable, str(script), "account", "--manifest", str(manifest),
373:             "--manifest-sha256", manifest_sha256,
374:             "--candidate-root", str(candidate), "--target-file", "SKILL.md",
375:         ],
376:         check=True, capture_output=True, text=True,
377:     )
378:     assert json.loads(account.stdout)["package"]["words"]["delta"] == -1

13: def test_package_mode_is_conditional_and_keeps_entrypoint_threshold() -> None:
14:     skill = (SKILL_DIR / "SKILL.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")

17:     assert "only when" in skill.lower()

24: def test_verdict_contract_requires_both_behavior_gates_and_never_reverts() -> None:
25:     skill = " ".join((SKILL_DIR / "SKILL.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8").split())
26:     protocol = " ".join(
27:         (SKILL_DIR / "references" / "package-resource-mode.md")
28:         .read_text(encoding="utf-8")
29:         .split()
30:     )
31: 
32:     # Pinned on the RESHAPE bullet's control keywords (Q1/Q3/Q2/marginal),
33:     # narrowed to that bullet's own window, rather than the exact prose
34:     # "Q1 and Q3 pass, Q2 marginal" -- a paraphrase of the same verdict
35:     # criteria ("Q1 and Q3 succeed while Q2 is marginal") would preserve
36:     # the RESHAPE contract untouched but fail a verbatim pin. Bounded
37:     # between the RESHAPE and REJECT bullets so the tokens must actually
38:     # co-occur in RESHAPE's own criteria, not just appear anywhere in the
39:     # three-way verdict list.
40:     reshape_window = re.search(
41:         r"\*\*RESHAPE\*\*(.*?)-\s*\*\*REJECT\*\*", skill
42:     )
43:     assert reshape_window, "RESHAPE verdict bullet not found between PROCEED and REJECT"
44:     window = reshape_window.group(1)
45:     for token in ("Q1", "Q3", "pass", "Q2", "marginal"):
46:         assert token in window, f"RESHAPE bullet must name {token!r}"
47:     assert "Or revert" not in skill
48:     assert "overall `PROCEED` verdict" in protocol
49:
